## Mark 1:1 (id: 21195)

* Mark begins with an introduction. This gospel is about Jesus. In each story in Mark, we should ask, “What is Mark teaching about Jesus in this passage?”

* *Gospel*: This is a common term in Mark (see [1:14–15](https://ref.ly/Mark1:14-Mark1:15); [8:35](https://ref.ly/Mark8:35); [10:29](https://ref.ly/Mark10:29); [13:10](https://ref.ly/Mark13:10); [14:9](https://ref.ly/Mark14:9)). It can also be translated as *good news*. The gospel is the good news about Jesus. See study note on Mark 1:15.
* The Hebrew word *mashiakh* (Messiah) has the same meaning as the Greek term *christos* (Christ). Both mean “anointed one.”

    In the Old Testament, the people of Israel anointed:

    + priests ([Exodus 28:41](https://ref.ly/Exod28:41); [Leviticus 16:32](https://ref.ly/Lev16:32); [21:10](https://ref.ly/Lev21:10)),
        + kings ([2 Samuel 1:14](https://ref.ly/2Sam1:14), [16](https://ref.ly/2Sam1:16); [19:21](https://ref.ly/2Sam19:21); [Psalm 2](https://ref.ly/Ps2:1-Ps2:12)), and
        + prophets ([1 Kings 19:16](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:16)).
        They anointed them with olive oil to show the Lord’s presence, blessing, and authority for the tasks God gave them.

    Over time, Israelites increasingly anticipated the Messiah, “the anointed one.” He would be a descendant of King David, who would be Israel’s king. The political associations of this title in the first century led Jesus to avoid openly declaring himself as the Messiah (see [Mark 3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12); [8:27–30](https://ref.ly/Mark8:27-Mark8:30); [14:61–63](https://ref.ly/Mark14:61-Mark14:63); [15:2](https://ref.ly/Mark15:2), [26](https://ref.ly/Mark15:26); [John 4:25–26](https://ref.ly/John4:25-John4:26)).

* *The Son of God*: This title emphasizes the special relationship between Jesus and God the Father ([Mark 1:11](https://ref.ly/Mark1:11); [9:7](https://ref.ly/Mark9:7); [12:4–6](https://ref.ly/Mark12:4-Mark12:6); [14:61–62](https://ref.ly/Mark14:61-Mark14:62)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:1
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 1:15 (ID: `21191@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`)

## Mark 1:2–3 (id: 21196)

* Mark includes Old Testament prophecies (messages from God given long ago) to support his account of the good news about Jesus Christ. John the Baptist’s role as a prophet and messenger shows that Jesus is the promised Lord and king. He is the Christ (or Messiah, God’s chosen one) and the Son of God.
* *Isaiah*: Mark follows the Jewish practice of mentioning only the best\-known source in a quotation. Here he names Isaiah, even though the quotation also includes ([Malachi 3:1](https://ref.ly/Mal3:1); [Isaiah 40:3](https://ref.ly/Isa40:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:2-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 1:2–8 (id: 21197)

This account does not focus mainly on John the Baptist, the messenger. Instead, it is about the one whom he announces as Jesus the Messiah, the Son of God ([1:1](https://ref.ly/Mark1:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:2-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); John (ID: `person:John`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Strap (ID: `realia:Strap`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Belt (ID: `realia:Belt`); Locust (ID: `fauna:Locust`); Leather (ID: `realia:Leather`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`); Bee (ID: `fauna:Bee`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`)

## Mark 1:3 (id: 21212)

John the Baptist was the *voice of one calling in the wilderness*, urging people to prepare for the Lord's coming. In the book of Isaiah, this prophecy speaks of the coming of the Lord, the God of Israel. Here, it refers to the Lord Jesus (see [12:35–37](https://ref.ly/Mark12:35-Mark12:37)). The early church called itself "the Way," likely referring to this promise (see [Acts 9:2](https://ref.ly/Acts9:2); [19:9](https://ref.ly/Acts19:9), [23](https://ref.ly/Acts19:23); [22:4](https://ref.ly/Acts22:4); [24:14](https://ref.ly/Acts24:14), [22](https://ref.ly/Acts24:22); compare [Acts 2:28](https://ref.ly/Acts2:28); [18:25–26](https://ref.ly/Acts18:25-Acts18:26); [Romans 2:20](https://ref.ly/Rom2:20); [2 Peter 2:2](https://ref.ly/2Pet2:2), [21](https://ref.ly/2Pet2:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Straightness (ID: `keyterm:Straightness`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 1:4 (id: 21216)

* John's mission as the Lord's messenger was to prepare people for the arrival of the Messiah (see [Luke 1:76–77](https://ref.ly/Luke1:76-Luke1:77)). He instructed them to confess their sins, seek God's forgiveness, and be baptized. Those who did this were ready to receive the message of Jesus (see [Luke 7:29–30](https://ref.ly/Luke7:29-Luke7:30)).
* John was in the wilderness, likely the desert area around the Jordan River north of the Dead Sea. This shows he was the "voice" Isaiah mentioned ([Mark 1:3](https://ref.ly/Mark1:3)).
* *preaching a baptism*: The exact origin of John's baptism is uncertain. The Qumran community practiced a "baptism" when someone became a part of the community, which they repeated regularly. But John's baptism was a one\-time event. Jewish baptism for those converting to Judaism is another possible source, but it is more likely that such baptisms were not practiced until after John's ministry.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`)

## Mark 1:5 (id: 21217)

*All of … Judea*: John the Baptist attracted a lot of attention. Many people believed that there had been no prophet for over 400 years. So, prophets were associated with Israel's past and the future reign of the Messiah. 

John preached repentance (turning away from sin and returning to God), like the ancient prophets. He also dressed like the great prophet Elijah ([1:6](https://ref.ly/Mark1:6)), whom God promised would return in the last days ([Malachi 4:5](https://ref.ly/Mal4:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Mark 1:6 (id: 21218)

* *camel’s hair, with a leather belt*: Compare with Elijah ([2 Kings 1:8](https://ref.ly/2Kgs1:8)).
* John ate what was available in the wilderness. Locusts (a type of insect) were allowed as food by God’s law (compare [Leviticus 11:20–24](https://ref.ly/Lev11:20-Lev11:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Bag (ID: `realia:Bag`); Belt (ID: `realia:Belt`); Locust (ID: `fauna:Locust`); Bee (ID: `fauna:Bee`); Camel (ID: `fauna:Camel`); Leather (ID: `realia:Leather`)

## Mark 1:7 (id: 21219)

*will come one*: John knew he was preparing for the coming of the Messiah, but he did not yet know that Jesus was the Messiah (see [Luke 7:18–23](https://ref.ly/Luke7:18-Luke7:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Strap (ID: `realia:Strap`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Sandal (ID: `realia:Sandal`)

## Mark 1:8 (id: 21220)

John’s baptism prepared people for God's kingdom by calling them to repentance. The baptism of Jesus brought the gift of the Holy Spirit*,* through whom sinful people become God’s children ([Romans 8:15–16](https://ref.ly/Rom8:15-Rom8:16); [1 Corinthians 12:13](https://ref.ly/1Cor12:13); [Galatians 4:6](https://ref.ly/Gal4:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`)

## Mark 1:9 (id: 21221)

* At this time, Jesus lived in Nazareth, a small town in lower Galilee ([Matthew 2:19–23](https://ref.ly/Matt2:19-Matt2:23); [Luke 2:39](https://ref.ly/Luke2:39)). Jesus soon left Nazareth for Capernaum, a city on the Sea of Galilee ([Matthew 4:13](https://ref.ly/Matt4:13)).
* *was baptized by John*: [Matthew 3:14–15](https://ref.ly/Matt3:14-Matt3:15) explains the reason Jesus received the baptism of repentance.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`); John (ID: `person:John`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Mark 1:10 (id: 21186)

* The phrase *the heavens breaking open* shows that Jesus has a unique and direct relationship with God the Father. In [Mark 15:38](https://ref.ly/Mark15:38), the same verb shows that believers also gain access to God through the death of Jesus.
* All four Gospels mention the Holy Spirit coming down on Jesus like a dove. The Spirit, who was active in the first creation, now works with Jesus to begin the new creation ([Genesis 1:2](https://ref.ly/Gen1:2); [Romans 8:15–17](https://ref.ly/Rom8:15-Rom8:17); [1 Corinthians 6:11](https://ref.ly/1Cor6:11); [Ephesians 1:13–14](https://ref.ly/Eph1:13-Eph1:14); [2 Thessalonians 2:13](https://ref.ly/2Thess2:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Dove (ID: `fauna:Dove`)

## Mark 1:11 (id: 21187)

*You are My beloved Son*: By age twelve, Jesus was aware of his unique relationship with God the Father ([Luke 2:49](https://ref.ly/Luke2:49)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Mark 1:12–13 (id: 21188)

* *the Spirit drove Jesus* (compare [Matthew 4:1](https://ref.ly/Matt4:1); [Luke 4:1](https://ref.ly/Luke4:1)): he Spirit led Jesus into the wilderness. From the beginning of his ministry, Jesus defeated Satan and resisted temptation. His later acts of casting out demons ([Mark 1:21–34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21-Mark1:34); [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12); [5:1–20](https://ref.ly/Mark5:1-Mark5:20); [9:14–27](https://ref.ly/Mark9:14-Mark9:27)) show the results of that victory (see [3:27](https://ref.ly/Mark3:27)).
* Jesus was tempted in the wilderness of Judea. In Scripture, the wilderness often has a sense of danger and evil because of Satan and wild animals ([Isaiah 13:19–22](https://ref.ly/Isa13:19-Isa13:22); [Ezekiel 34:25](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:25)). These animals included dogs, wolves, leopards, jackals, and bears.
* The *forty days* remind us of Israel's forty years of testing in the wilderness. Israel failed, but Jesus succeeded. He resisted temptation without sinning ([Hebrews 2:18](https://ref.ly/Heb2:18); [4:15](https://ref.ly/Heb4:15); see also [James 1:3](https://ref.ly/Jas1:3), [12](https://ref.ly/Jas1:12); [1 Peter 1:7](https://ref.ly/1Pet1:7); [Revelation 2:10](https://ref.ly/Rev2:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:12-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Put to the Test (ID: `keyterm:PutToTheTest`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`)

## Mark 1:14 (id: 21190)

Jesus began his ministry after John was arrested by Herod Antipas (the ruler of Galilee). There was some overlap between their ministries ([John 3:22–24](https://ref.ly/John3:22-John3:24); [4:1–2](https://ref.ly/John4:1-John4:2)), but most of the ministry of Jesus happened after John’s.

John belongs mainly to the old order (the time before the full ministry of Jesus; [Matthew 11:7–14](https://ref.ly/Matt11:7-Matt11:14)), while Jesus brings the new. Both men carried out God’s plan, and both were arrested and put to death ([Mark 6:14–29](https://ref.ly/Mark6:14-Mark6:29); [9:31](https://ref.ly/Mark9:31); [10:33](https://ref.ly/Mark10:33)). 

Jesus started preaching in Galilee after John's arrest. But [John 2:13–4:43](https://ref.ly/John2:13-John4:43) suggests Jesus had an earlier ministry in Judea.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); John (ID: `person:John`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 1:14–15 (id: 21189)

This summary introduces [1:14–3:6](https://ref.ly/Mark1:14-Mark3:6). Such summaries help hearers understand what follows (see also [3:7–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:7-Mark3:12); [6:6](https://ref.ly/Mark6:6)). Most of Mark’s original audience would have heard the Gospel read aloud.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); John (ID: `person:John`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Mark 1:15 (id: 21191)

* The preaching of Jesus is summarized in his announcement that the kingdom of God had come. He called people to repent (turn away from sin and turn to God) and believe the good news about the Messiah ([1:1](https://ref.ly/Mark1:1)).
* The kingdom of God is not associated with a place or land. It began with Jesus himself and continues through his followers.
* Because the kingdom of God has come, people must repent and have faith in God's good news. In the time of Jesus, this good news meant that God’s reign had arrived through the Messiah. For Mark’s later audience, it also included the news of the death, resurrection, ascension of Jesus, and his promised return ([15:1–16:8](https://ref.ly/Mark15:1-Mark16:8)).

    All people, both Jews and non\-Jews (called Gentiles), need God's forgiveness through repentance and faith in Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Repent (ID: `keyterm:Repent`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 1:16–20 (id: 21192)

The call of the two pairs of brothers (Simon and Andrew, and James and John) came after they had already met Jesus earlier ([John 1:35–42](https://ref.ly/John1:35-John1:42)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:16-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Zebedee (ID: `person:Zebedee`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Casting Net (ID: `realia:CastingNet`); Fishnet (ID: `realia:Fishnet`); Sea of Galilee (ID: `place:GalileeSea`); James (ID: `person:James`); Hired Worker (ID: `keyterm:HiredWorker`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Andrew (ID: `person:Andrew`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Mark 1:17 (id: 21193)

Mark uses the invitation of Jesus, *Come, follow Me* ([1:20](https://ref.ly/Mark1:20); [8:34](https://ref.ly/Mark8:34)) and the repeated idea of people following him ([1:18](https://ref.ly/Mark1:18); [2:14–15](https://ref.ly/Mark2:14-Mark2:15); [3:7](https://ref.ly/Mark3:7); [5:24](https://ref.ly/Mark5:24)) to describe what it means to be his disciple (see “Following Jesus” Theme Note).

## Mark 1:21 (id: 21199)

Mark begins his first account of an exorcism (driving or casting out an evil spirit) with Jesus and his companions going into the synagogue in Capernaum. Jesus teaching there suggests that he was already known as a teacher, so he was invited to speak (compare [Acts 13:15](https://ref.ly/Acts13:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`)

## Mark 1:21–28 (id: 21198)

Jesus driving out evil spirits shows who he is and his power over Satan (see study notes on Mark 1:12–13 and 3:27; see also [1:34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:34), [39](https://ref.ly/Mark1:39); [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12), [22](https://ref.ly/Mark3:22); [5:1–20](https://ref.ly/Mark5:1-Mark5:20); [6:13](https://ref.ly/Mark6:13); [7:24–30](https://ref.ly/Mark7:24-Mark7:30); [9:14–29](https://ref.ly/Mark9:14-Mark9:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:21-28

## Mark 1:22 (id: 21200)

The teachers of religious law were professional scribes (scholars who taught, copied, and interpreted Jewish law for the people). They were primarily associated with the Pharisees. The authority of the scribes came from quoting other scribes (compare [Matthew 5:21–48](https://ref.ly/Matt5:21-Matt5:48)). When Jesus taught, the people were amazed because he spoke and acted with real authority (see [Mark 1:27](https://ref.ly/Mark1:27); [5:20](https://ref.ly/Mark5:20); [6:2](https://ref.ly/Mark6:2); [7:37](https://ref.ly/Mark7:37); [10:24–32](https://ref.ly/Mark10:24-Mark10:32); [11:18](https://ref.ly/Mark11:18); [15:5](https://ref.ly/Mark15:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Mark 1:23–24 (id: 21201)

* The demon spoke through the man who was possessed by an evil spirit ([1:24](https://ref.ly/Mark1:24)). In the Gospels, demons are often associated with disease ([9:17](https://ref.ly/Mark9:17), [27](https://ref.ly/Mark9:27); [Matthew 17:15](https://ref.ly/Matt17:15)) and self\-destructive behavior ([Mark 1:26](https://ref.ly/Mark1:26); [5:2–5](https://ref.ly/Mark5:2-Mark5:5); [9:17–18](https://ref.ly/Mark9:17-Mark9:18), [20–22](https://ref.ly/Mark9:20-Mark9:22)).

    Demons know who Jesus is ([1:34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:34)). They constantly identify him as *the Holy One of God* (for similar phrases see [3:11](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11); [5:7](https://ref.ly/Mark5:7)).

* In saying *us,* the evil spirit spoke on behalf of all demons. It recognized that Jesus has full authority and understood that he had come to oppose and destroy evil.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:23-24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Nazareth (ID: `place:Nazareth`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Mark 1:25 (id: 21202)

* Jesus did not need to shout or use magic words. He spoke with the authority of the Son of God, and the evil spirit obeyed ([1:1](https://ref.ly/Mark1:1)).
* *Be silent!*: Jesus commanded the evil spirit not to reveal his identity. This is an example of the “messianic secret.” This term describes places in Mark where Jesus tells demons or people not to reveal who he is ([1:25](https://ref.ly/Mark1:25), [34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:34); [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12); [8:30](https://ref.ly/Mark8:30); [9:9](https://ref.ly/Mark9:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Mark 1:26 (id: 21203)

The *loud shriek* and *convulsions* caused by the *unclean spirit* were cries of defeat as it left the man (see also [1:27](https://ref.ly/Mark1:27); [5:13](https://ref.ly/Mark5:13); [9:26](https://ref.ly/Mark9:26); compare [15:37](https://ref.ly/Mark15:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:26


## Mark 1:27–28 (id: 21204)

* The amazement caused by this exorcism quickly led to the spread of the news about Jesus.
* *What is this? A new teaching with authority!*: People saw the healings and exorcisms that Jesus did as being part of his teaching ([1:21–22](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21-Mark1:22), [27](https://ref.ly/Mark1:27); compare [1:38–39](https://ref.ly/Mark1:38-Mark1:39); [3:14–15](https://ref.ly/Mark3:14-Mark3:15); [6:2](https://ref.ly/Mark6:2), [5](https://ref.ly/Mark6:5), [12–13](https://ref.ly/Mark6:12-Mark6:13), [30](https://ref.ly/Mark6:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:27-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`)

Simon, also called Peter, was married (see [1 Corinthians 9:5](https://ref.ly/1Cor9:5)).

## Mark 1:31 (id: 21206)

Jesus healed Simon’s mother\-in\-law immediately (see also [1:40–45](https://ref.ly/Mark1:40-Mark1:45); [2:1–12](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1-Mark2:12); [3:1–6](https://ref.ly/Mark3:1-Mark3:6); [5:25–34](https://ref.ly/Mark5:25-Mark5:34); [7:31–37](https://ref.ly/Mark7:31-Mark7:37); [8:22–26](https://ref.ly/Mark8:22-Mark8:26); [10:46–52](https://ref.ly/Mark10:46-Mark10:52)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`)

## Mark 1:32–34 (id: 21207)

These public healings happened after sunset, following the Sabbath day ([1:21](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21), [29](https://ref.ly/Mark1:29)). Many people witnessed them (see also [1:39](https://ref.ly/Mark1:39); [3:10–11](https://ref.ly/Mark3:10-Mark3:11); [6:5](https://ref.ly/Mark6:5), [53–56](https://ref.ly/Mark6:53-Mark6:56)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:32-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Demon Possessed (ID: `keyterm:DemonPossessed`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`)

## Mark 1:33 (id: 21208)

Jesus became more popular with the crowds (also in [1:37](https://ref.ly/Mark1:37), [39](https://ref.ly/Mark1:39), [45](https://ref.ly/Mark1:45)). Meanwhile, the leaders increasingly rejected him ([2:1–3:6](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1-Mark3:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:33


## Mark 1:34 (id: 21209)

*they knew who He was*: See [1:23–25](https://ref.ly/Mark1:23-Mark1:25).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`)

## Mark 1:35 (id: 21210)

Despite his busy ministry, Jesus made time to pray ([6:46](https://ref.ly/Mark6:46); [14:32–39](https://ref.ly/Mark14:32-Mark14:39)). Mark emphasizes how important prayer was in the life of Jesus (see also [Luke 3:21](https://ref.ly/Luke3:21); [6:12](https://ref.ly/Luke6:12); [9:18](https://ref.ly/Luke9:18), [28–29](https://ref.ly/Luke9:28-Luke9:29); [11:1–4](https://ref.ly/Luke11:1-Luke11:4); [18:1](https://ref.ly/Luke18:1); [22:32](https://ref.ly/Luke22:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`)

## Mark 1:38–39 (id: 21211)

* Prayer equipped Jesus to preach, teach, heal, and drive out demons ([1:35](https://ref.ly/Mark1:35)).
* The mission of Jesus in Galilee focused on preaching in the synagogues, where he took the opportunity to speak ([1:21](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21); [Luke 4:16–30](https://ref.ly/Luke4:16-Luke4:30)). The apostle Paul later took advantage of this same opportunity ([Acts 13:5](https://ref.ly/Acts13:5), [14–15](https://ref.ly/Acts13:14-Acts13:15); [14:1](https://ref.ly/Acts14:1); [16:13](https://ref.ly/Acts16:13); [17:1](https://ref.ly/Acts17:1)).
* Jesus relied on the Jewish practice of kindness to strangers when he traveled (compare [Mark 6:10](https://ref.ly/Mark6:10); [Genesis 18:1–8](https://ref.ly/Gen18:1-Gen18:8); [19:1–8](https://ref.ly/Gen19:1-Gen19:8); [Luke 10:7](https://ref.ly/Luke10:7); [Hebrews 13:2](https://ref.ly/Heb13:2); [3 John 1:5–8](https://ref.ly/3John1:5-3John1:8)).
* The region of Galilee measured about 65 kilometers (40 miles) north to south and 40 kilometers (25 miles) east to west. In the time of Jesus, Galilee was ruled by Herod Antipas.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:38-39

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Mark 1:40 (id: 21213)

*Leprosy* refers to several skin diseases (or even mildew in a building). An infected person was considered ceremonially unclean and was excluded from family and society ([Leviticus 13:45–46](https://ref.ly/Lev13:45-Lev13:46); [Numbers 12:9–12](https://ref.ly/Num12:9-Num12:12); [2 Chronicles 26:16–21](https://ref.ly/2Chr26:16-2Chr26:21); [Luke 17:12](https://ref.ly/Luke17:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Mark 1:41 (id: 21214)

Jesus was willing to touch a ceremonially unclean leper. This act shows the new order of the kingdom of God ([1:15](https://ref.ly/Mark1:15); [5:25–34](https://ref.ly/Mark5:25-Mark5:34); [Luke 7:36–50](https://ref.ly/Luke7:36-Luke7:50); see [Matthew 10:8](https://ref.ly/Matt10:8); [11:5](https://ref.ly/Matt11:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`)

## Mark 1:43–45 (id: 21215)

* Jesus instructed the man to visit the priest to confirm his healing and make the required sacrificial offering. This would act as public proof of his healing.
* Despite the severe warning Jesus gave, the man went and told people what happened to him. As a result, Jesus could not enter the town and had to preach in more remote places. Jesus became widely known, and people from many places came to see him ([3:7–8](https://ref.ly/Mark3:7-Mark3:8)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 1:43-45

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`)

## Mark 2:1 (id: 21435)

*He was home*: See [Matthew 4:13](https://ref.ly/Matt4:13). The large crowd shows that Jesus was popular.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 2:1–12 (id: 21426)

This controversy story is also a miracle story. It includes both a dispute and an act of healing or power.

Because it contains a miracle, it connects with the earlier group of miracle stories in [1:21–45](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21-Mark1:45).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:1-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`)

## Mark 2:1–3.6 (id: 21427)

Five controversy stories ([2:1–12](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1-Mark2:12), [13–17](https://ref.ly/Mark2:13-Mark2:17), [18–22](https://ref.ly/Mark2:18-Mark2:22), [23–28](https://ref.ly/Mark2:23-Mark2:28); [3:1–6](https://ref.ly/Mark3:1-Mark3:6)) are grouped together because they share a common theme. Each story shows a conflict between Jesus and the religious leaders. In these accounts, Jesus says or does things that challenge their beliefs and practices. These stories show the great authority that Jesus has.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:1-3:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Abiathar (ID: `person:Abiathar`); Alphaeus (ID: `person:Alphaeus.2`); Herodians (ID: `group:Herodian`); Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Tax Office (ID: `keyterm:TaxOffice`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Be Healthy (ID: `keyterm:Healthy`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); David (ID: `person:David`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); John (ID: `person:John`); Patch (ID: `realia:Patch`); Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`); Skin-Bottle (ID: `realia:Skin-bottle`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`)

## Mark 2:4 (id: 21440)

* *through the crowd*: See [2:1](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1).
* Houses at that time usually had flat roofs made of mud, thatch (dry plant material), and branches. The men made an opening by digging through the roof.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Housetop (ID: `realia:Housetop`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Mark 2:5 (id: 21441)

* Jesus saw the faith of *the* *paralytic* and the four men who brought him.
* *your sins are forgiven*: Those listening understood the words of Jesus as a claim to having the same authority as God (see [2:6–10](https://ref.ly/Mark2:6-Mark2:10); [Luke 7:48–49](https://ref.ly/Luke7:48-Luke7:49)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`)

## Mark 2:6–7 (id: 21442)

Only God can forgive sins*,* yet Jesus had authority to do so ([2:10](https://ref.ly/Mark2:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 2:9–11 (id: 21443)

*Which is easier: to say … 'Your sins are forgiven'*: This claim cannot be directly proved or disproved, while the claim to heal can be. Jesus showed his ability to heal in order to show his authority to forgive.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:9-11

## Mark 2:12 (id: 21428)

*We have never seen anything like this!*:Mark often shows how Jesus amazed people ([1:22](https://ref.ly/Mark1:22), [27](https://ref.ly/Mark1:27); [4:41](https://ref.ly/Mark4:41); [6:50–51](https://ref.ly/Mark6:50-Mark6:51)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Mark 2:13–15 (id: 21429)

* Levi was also called Matthew (see [Matthew 9:9–10](https://ref.ly/Matt9:9-Matt9:10)). Certain people are known by two names in the New Testament (for example, Simon is also called Peter; Saul is also called Paul; Judas son of James is also called Thaddaeus, see study note on Mark 3:18).
* This type of tax collector (Greek *telōnēs*) collected sales taxes, customs, and road tolls, unlike those who collected the poll tax ([12:14](https://ref.ly/Mark12:14)). Other Jews disliked local tax collectors like Levi because they often charged the public more than they owed ([Luke 19:8](https://ref.ly/Luke19:8)). As agents of the Romans, the Jews considered them to be traitors. Jesus invited Levi, a tax collector, to follow him, joining Peter, Andrew, James, and John ([Mark 1:16–20](https://ref.ly/Mark1:16-Mark1:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:13-15
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 3:18 (ID: `21452@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Alphaeus (ID: `person:Alphaeus.2`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Tax Office (ID: `keyterm:TaxOffice`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 2:13–17 (id: 21430)

This controversy is about Jesus spending time with sinners who had a bad reputation, such as tax collectors, and eating with them.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:13-17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Alphaeus (ID: `person:Alphaeus.2`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Tax Office (ID: `keyterm:TaxOffice`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Be Healthy (ID: `keyterm:Healthy`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 2:16 (id: 21431)

The Pharisees, who were teachers of religious law, regarded Jesus eating with sinners as him accepting them as friends ([Luke 15:1–2](https://ref.ly/Luke15:1-Luke15:2); [19:5](https://ref.ly/Luke19:5), [7](https://ref.ly/Luke19:7); [Acts 11:2–18](https://ref.ly/Acts11:2-Acts11:18); [Galatians 2:11–12](https://ref.ly/Gal2:11-Gal2:12)). Jesus showed that these tax collectors and sinners were welcome in the kingdom of God. 

In his ministry, Jesus invited both Jews and Gentiles to share in the kingdom of God ([Mark 5:1–20](https://ref.ly/Mark5:1-Mark5:20); [7:24–30](https://ref.ly/Mark7:24-Mark7:30); compare [Matthew 8:5–13](https://ref.ly/Matt8:5-Matt8:13); [Luke 7:1–10](https://ref.ly/Luke7:1-Luke7:10); [John 4:43–54](https://ref.ly/John4:43-John4:54)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Tax Collector (ID: `keyterm:TaxCollector`)

## Mark 2:17 (id: 21432)

Jesus answered his opponents with a well\-known proverb. He compared himself to a doctor and sinners to sick people. Salvation is for those who know they are sinners, not for those who think they are righteous ([Luke 16:15](https://ref.ly/Luke16:15); [18:9](https://ref.ly/Luke18:9); [Romans 3:23](https://ref.ly/Rom3:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Healthy (ID: `keyterm:Healthy`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`)

## Mark 2:18–22 (id: 21433)

This controversy deals with fasting*,* which Jesus did not practice with his disciples. The Pharisees regularly fasted two days a week, on Mondays and Thursdays ([Luke 18:12](https://ref.ly/Luke18:12)). Jews often fasted when mourning or specially seeking the Lord's favor ([Leviticus 16:29–31](https://ref.ly/Lev16:29-Lev16:31); [1 Samuel 31:13](https://ref.ly/1Sam31:13); [2 Samuel 1:12](https://ref.ly/2Sam1:12); [12:21–23](https://ref.ly/2Sam12:21-2Sam12:23); [Ezra 8:23](https://ref.ly/Ezra8:23); [Esther 4:3](https://ref.ly/Esth4:3); [Matthew 6:16](https://ref.ly/Matt6:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:18-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); John (ID: `person:John`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Patch (ID: `realia:Patch`); Skin-Bottle (ID: `realia:Skin-bottle`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`)

## Mark 2:19–20 (id: 21434)

Jesus was like a *bridegroom* at his wedding, so fasting was not appropriate. It was a time to celebrate ([Luke 15:23–25](https://ref.ly/Luke15:23-Luke15:25), [32](https://ref.ly/Luke15:32)). The disciples would fast after Jesus went up to heaven (see [Acts 13:2–3](https://ref.ly/Acts13:2-Acts13:3); [14:23](https://ref.ly/Acts14:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abstain from Food (ID: `keyterm:Fast`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Mark 2:21–22 (id: 21436)

The old customs of the old covenant do not fit with the new arrival of God's kingdom. People know that a *patch of unshrunk* *cloth* sewn on old clothing will tear the old cloth as it shrinks. In the same way, cracking *old wineskins* will burst when new wine ferments in them. The fasting of the old ways cannot be mixed with the feasting that comes with the new.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Patch (ID: `realia:Patch`); Skin-Bottle (ID: `realia:Skin-bottle`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`)

## Mark 2:23–28 (id: 21437)

In this controversy, Jesus and his disciples are accused of breaking the Sabbath ([Exodus 20:8–11](https://ref.ly/Exod20:8-Exod20:11)). Picking grain by hand in another person’s field was lawful ([Deuteronomy 23:25](https://ref.ly/Deut23:25)). The charge was that by rubbing the chaff from the kernels, the disciples were working on the Sabbath*,* which was forbidden ([Exodus 34:21](https://ref.ly/Exod34:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:23-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Abiathar (ID: `person:Abiathar`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); David (ID: `person:David`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Mark 2:25–26 (id: 21438)

Jesus answered by referring to an event in the life of King David ([1 Samuel 21](https://ref.ly/1Sam21:1-1Sam21:15)). David and his men ate the sacred bread, the twelve loaves in the tabernacle that only priests were allowed to eat (see [Leviticus 24:5–9](https://ref.ly/Lev24:5-Lev24:9)). In that sense, they did what the law did not permit. But Jesus showed that a deeper principle was also at work ([Mark 2:27–28](https://ref.ly/Mark2:27-Mark2:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:25-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Abiathar (ID: `person:Abiathar`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); David (ID: `person:David`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Mark 2:27–28 (id: 21439)

Since Jesus is the Lord of humanity ([1 Corinthians 15:25–28](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:25-1Cor15:28); [Ephesians 1:20–22](https://ref.ly/Eph1:20-Eph1:22); [Philippians 2:9–11](https://ref.ly/Phil2:9-Phil2:11)) and since the Sabbath was made to meet the needs of people*,* Jesus is *Lord even of the Sabbath*. Like when Jesus claimed authority to forgive sins, this also showed that he claimed authority that belongs to God ([Mark 2:7](https://ref.ly/Mark2:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 2:27-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`)

## Mark 3:1–2 (id: 21444)

The scene again involves a synagogue*,* probably in Capernaum ([1:21](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21), [29](https://ref.ly/Mark1:29)). Theman with a withered hand was present on the Sabbath ([3:1–2](https://ref.ly/Mark3:1-Mark3:2)). The enemies of Jesus wanted to use this situation against him. If he healed the man, they could accuse him, so they watched him closely.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Mark 3:1–6 (id: 21445)

This story ends the collection of controversy stories ([2:1–3:6](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1-Mark3:6)). Like the previous story, Jesus argues with the Pharisees about the Sabbath. As in the first story, the controversy involves a healing ([2:1–12](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1-Mark2:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Herodians (ID: `group:Herodian`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Mark 3:3–4 (id: 21461)

* Jesus challenged his enemies' belief that God did not allow doing good works on the Sabbath (see [Luke 13:10–17](https://ref.ly/Luke13:10-Luke13:17)).
* *to save life or to destroy it*: This might refer to an incident in which the Maccabees decided to fight if attacked on the Sabbath (*1 Maccabees* 2:32–41\). These great Jewish heroes were willing to kill on the Sabbath day. But the Pharisees did not allow good works on that day. The question Jesus asked embarrassed them. It shamed them into silence, but they still refused to change ([Mark 3:5](https://ref.ly/Mark3:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:3-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Mark 3:5–6 (id: 21465)

Those who opposed Jesus were stubborn and would not listen to good reasoning. Instead of changing, they began to plan how to kill him (see also [11:18](https://ref.ly/Mark11:18); [12:12](https://ref.ly/Mark12:12); [14:1–2](https://ref.ly/Mark14:1-Mark14:2), [10–11](https://ref.ly/Mark14:10-Mark14:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Herodians (ID: `group:Herodian`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`)

## Mark 3:7–12 (id: 21466)

* Jesus went to the lake (the Sea of Galilee), which prepares for the events in [4:1–5:43](https://ref.ly/Mark4:1-Mark5:43). The large crowd that came to see John the Baptist ([1:7](https://ref.ly/Mark1:7)) was smaller than the crowd that came to see Jesus.
* Idumea was a region in southern Judea, settled by the Edomites after the exile in Babylon.
* Tyre and Sidon were Phoenician cities on the Mediterranean coast that Jesus later visited ([7:24](https://ref.ly/Mark7:24), [31](https://ref.ly/Mark7:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:7-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Idumea (ID: `place:Idumea`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Jordan River (ID: `place:Jordan`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`); Judea (ID: `place:Judea`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Mark 3:9 (id: 21467)

The boat likely belonged to Peter and Andrew or James and John. The crowd wanted to see Jesus so much that he needed to avoid being crushed by the people ([4:1](https://ref.ly/Mark4:1); [5:24](https://ref.ly/Mark5:24), [31](https://ref.ly/Mark5:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Mark 3:10 (id: 21446)

Jesus healed many people that day. He might have healed all the sick (see [Matthew 12:15](https://ref.ly/Matt12:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`)

## Mark 3:11–12 (id: 21447)

The *unclean spirits* knew who Jesus was ([1:34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:34); see also [1:22](https://ref.ly/Mark1:22); [5:7](https://ref.ly/Mark5:7)). But Jesus did not want people to know his identity.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`)

## Mark 3:13–19 (id: 21448)

The scene shifts to a mountain. Mark describes how Jesus called his disciples to come to him. This is similar to God's call to Old Testament prophets ([Exodus 3:14–22](https://ref.ly/Exod3:14-Exod3:22); [1 Samuel 3:4–14](https://ref.ly/1Sam3:4-1Sam3:14); [Jeremiah 1:5–19](https://ref.ly/Jer1:5-Jer1:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:13-19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Bartholomew (ID: `person:Bartholomew`); Zealots (ID: `group:Zealot`); James (ID: `person:James`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); James (ID: `person:James.2`); Alphaeus (ID: `person:Alphaeus`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`); Thomas (ID: `person:Thomas`); Andrew (ID: `person:Andrew`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Zebedee (ID: `person:Zebedee`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.2`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas`); Philip (ID: `person:Philip`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`)

## Mark 3:14–15 (id: 21449)

Jesus chose 12 men to represent Israel's 12 tribes and to announce that the kingdom of God had arrived. He called them apostles. The Greek word *apostoloi* refers to people sent on a special mission. 

Jesus chose them to travel with him and to be sent out (Greek *apostellō*) to preach and cast out demons. They represented Jesus and had his authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:14-15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`)

## Mark 3:16–18 (id: 21450)

* Simon and Andrew were brothers, just like James and John ([1:16–20](https://ref.ly/Mark1:16-Mark1:20)).
* *Peter* is the Greek version of the Aramaic word *kepa’*, which means “rock.”

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:16-18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Bartholomew (ID: `person:Bartholomew`); James (ID: `person:James`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); James (ID: `person:James.2`); Alphaeus (ID: `person:Alphaeus`); Zealots (ID: `group:Zealot`); Zebedee (ID: `person:Zebedee`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.2`); Andrew (ID: `person:Andrew`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Philip (ID: `person:Philip`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Thomas (ID: `person:Thomas`)

## Mark 3:17 (id: 21451)

*whom He named Boanerges, meaning “Sons of Thunder”*: This likely describes their character. It may suggest strong emotions, boldness, or quick reactions. For example, they once wanted to call down fire on a village that rejected Jesus (see [Luke 9:53–54](https://ref.ly/Luke9:53-Luke9:54)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John.2`); James (ID: `person:James`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Zebedee (ID: `person:Zebedee`)

## Mark 3:18 (id: 21452)

Philip is fifth on all four lists of the twelve disciples ([Matthew 10:1–4](https://ref.ly/Matt10:1-Matt10:4); [Luke 6:12–16](https://ref.ly/Luke6:12-Luke6:16); [Acts 1:13](https://ref.ly/Acts1:13)). James son of Alphaeus (not the son of Zebedee and brother of John) is always ninth. Bartholomew might be Nathanael ([John 1:43–51](https://ref.ly/John1:43-John1:51)). Matthew is probably Levi, the tax collector ([Mark 2:14](https://ref.ly/Mark2:14); [Matthew 9:9](https://ref.ly/Matt9:9)). Thomas is also called Didymus (a Greek word meaning “twin,” [John 11:16](https://ref.ly/John11:16); [20:24](https://ref.ly/John20:24); [21:2](https://ref.ly/John21:2)). The New Testament does not explain this name further. Thaddaeus is missing from Luke’s list ([Matthew 10:3](https://ref.ly/Matt10:3)). He might be Judas, the son of James ([Luke 6:16](https://ref.ly/Luke6:16); [Acts 1:13](https://ref.ly/Acts1:13)). 

The presence of a government official (Matthew) and an anti\-government activist (Simon the Zealot) among the twelve apostles shows the ability of Jesus to change people and overcome the natural hostility between them.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** James (ID: `person:James.2`); Alphaeus (ID: `person:Alphaeus`); Bartholomew (ID: `person:Bartholomew`); Zealots (ID: `group:Zealot`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.2`); Andrew (ID: `person:Andrew`); Judas (ID: `person:Judas`); Matthew (ID: `person:Matthew`); Philip (ID: `person:Philip`); Thomas (ID: `person:Thomas`)

## Mark 3:19 (id: 21453)

* Identifying Judas as *Iscariot* suggests that Mark wanted to avoid confusing him with another Judas ([Luke 6:16](https://ref.ly/Luke6:16); [Acts 1:13](https://ref.ly/Acts1:13)). The name *Iscariot* likely comes from the Hebrew phrase *’ish qeriyoth*, meaning “man of Kerioth” (a town in Judea).
* *who betrayed Jesus*: See [Mark 14:10–11](https://ref.ly/Mark14:10-Mark14:11), [43–46](https://ref.ly/Mark14:43-Mark14:46).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Judas (ID: `person:Judas.2`)

## Mark 3:20 (id: 21455)

Jesus and his disciples could not even find time to eat. This shows how large the crowds were and how popular Jesus had become ([3:7–9](https://ref.ly/Mark3:7-Mark3:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 3:20–35 (id: 21454)

This story about Jesus and his family ([3:20–21](https://ref.ly/Mark3:20-Mark3:21) and [31–35](https://ref.ly/Mark3:31-Mark3:35)) surrounds a controversy story about Jesus driving out demons ([3:22–30](https://ref.ly/Mark3:22-Mark3:30)). This arrangement connects his family with the religious leaders, since both groups misunderstand him and stand against him.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:20-35

(ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Mark 3:21 (id: 21456)

*He is out of his mind*: Mark does not explain whether the attempt by the family of Jesus to seize him was motivated by sincere but misguided concern, or by hostility. The brothers and sisters of Jesus were not among his followers until after his resurrection ([3:31–35](https://ref.ly/Mark3:31-Mark3:35); [John 7:3–5](https://ref.ly/John7:3-John7:5)). [First Corinthians 15:7](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:7) mentions James, the brother of Jesus, who became a leader of the Jerusalem church ([Acts 15:13–21](https://ref.ly/Acts15:13-Acts15:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:21


## Mark 3:22 (id: 21457)

The *scribes* … *from Jerusalem* claimed Jesus was crazy because they believed Satan possessed him (see study note on 1:22) (Greek *Beelzeboul;* see study note on Matthew 10:25). The opponents of Jesus said his ability to perform miracles came from Satan (see the Jewish rabbinic work, *Sanhedrin* 3:43a, where they credit the working of miracles to magic).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:22
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 1:22 (ID: `21200@TyndaleStudyNotes`); Matthew 10:25 (ID: `21666@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`)

## Mark 3:23–27 (id: 21458)

Jesus showed that this charge made no sense by using several examples. Satan would not work against himself.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:23-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 3:27 (id: 21459)

Jesus explained his power to drive out demons by saying someone stronger than Satan had arrived and could take control of Satan’s house ([1:7](https://ref.ly/Mark1:7)). Jesus, the Messiah and the Son of God, was introducing God's kingdom and rescuing people from Satan's power ([1:1](https://ref.ly/Mark1:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 3:28–30 (id: 21460)

* The phrase *Truly* *I tell you* often introduces the sayings of Jesus and emphasizes their importance (see also [8:12](https://ref.ly/Mark8:12); [9:1](https://ref.ly/Mark9:1), [41](https://ref.ly/Mark9:41); [10:15](https://ref.ly/Mark10:15), [29](https://ref.ly/Mark10:29); [11:23](https://ref.ly/Mark11:23); [12:43](https://ref.ly/Mark12:43); [13:30](https://ref.ly/Mark13:30); [14:9](https://ref.ly/Mark14:9), [18](https://ref.ly/Mark14:18), [25](https://ref.ly/Mark14:25), [30](https://ref.ly/Mark14:30)).
* *the sons of men will be forgiven all sins and blasphemies*:One sin has eternal consequences. This is blasphemy against the Holy Spirit. To blaspheme the Holy Spirit means attributing the work of God to an *unclean spirit* (an evil spirit). Resisting and rejecting God's work in this way prevents the convicting work of the Spirit, which leads to repentance, faith in God, and forgiveness of sin (see study notes on Matthew 12:31–32; compare [Hebrews 6:4–6](https://ref.ly/Heb6:4-Heb6:6); [1 John 5:16–17](https://ref.ly/1John5:16-1John5:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:28-30
* **Associated Articles:** Matthew 12:31 (ID: `21719@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Holy Spirit (ID: `deity:HolySpirit`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`)

## Mark 3:31–35 (id: 21462)

* Mark returns to the story from [3:20–21](https://ref.ly/Mark3:20-Mark3:21).
* *Your mother and brothers*: Many people understand the brothers of Jesus to be children born to Joseph and Mary after Jesus was born (see also [6:3](https://ref.ly/Mark6:3); [Matthew 1:25](https://ref.ly/Matt1:25); [12:46](https://ref.ly/Matt12:46); [John 19:26–27](https://ref.ly/John19:26-John19:27)). Some interpreters think they were Joseph’s children from an earlier marriage or the cousins of Jesus. They hold this view because of the Roman Catholic belief that Mary remained a virgin for her whole life.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:31-35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 3:33–34 (id: 21463)

In God’s Kingdom, one’s true relatives are determined not by blood but by a faith relationship (see also [10:29–30](https://ref.ly/Mark10:29-Mark10:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:33-34


## Mark 3:35 (id: 21464)

Doing God's will includes repentance from sin, faith in God, and following Jesus ([1:15](https://ref.ly/Mark1:15), [18](https://ref.ly/Mark1:18), [20](https://ref.ly/Mark1:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 3:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

Jesus again uses a boat to avoid the press of the crowd when he is teaching ([3:9](https://ref.ly/Mark3:9); [Luke 5:2–3](https://ref.ly/Luke5:2-Luke5:3)).

## Mark 4:1–34 (id: 21468)

In this section, Mark gathers several teaching parables of Jesus. A parable is a short story that teaches a spiritual truth.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:1-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Acacia (ID: `flora:Acacia`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Mustard (ID: `flora:Mustard`); Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Sickle (ID: `realia:Sickle`); Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Mark 4:2 (id: 21481)

Parables are often stories ([Luke 15:11–32](https://ref.ly/Luke15:11-Luke15:32); [18:1–8](https://ref.ly/Luke18:1-Luke18:8)) but can also be:

* proverbs (short, wise sayings that teach a general truth; [Mark 3:24–25](https://ref.ly/Mark3:24-Mark3:25); [Luke 4:23](https://ref.ly/Luke4:23)),
* similes and metaphors ([Matthew 5:14](https://ref.ly/Matt5:14); [10:16](https://ref.ly/Matt10:16)),
* riddles ([Mark 7:15](https://ref.ly/Mark7:15); [14:58](https://ref.ly/Mark14:58)),
* comparisons ([Matthew 13:33](https://ref.ly/Matt13:33); [Luke 15:3–7](https://ref.ly/Luke15:3-Luke15:7)),
* examples ([Luke 10:30–35](https://ref.ly/Luke10:30-Luke10:35); [12:16–21](https://ref.ly/Luke12:16-Luke12:21)), or
* allegories (metaphors with specific symbols representing each part of the story; [Mark 4:3–9](https://ref.ly/Mark4:3-Mark4:9); [12:1–12](https://ref.ly/Mark12:1-Mark12:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`)

## Mark 4:3–9 (id: 21482)

The collection begins with the longest parable and its interpretation ([4:13–20](https://ref.ly/Mark4:13-Mark4:20)). The different types of soil represent different ways people respond to God’s message.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:3-9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Acacia (ID: `flora:Acacia`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Rooster (ID: `fauna:Rooster`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Bird (ID: `fauna:Bird`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Mark 4:10 (id: 21469)

The scene changes from Jesus teaching a large crowd from a boat to being alone with *the Twelve and those around Him*. They ask Jesus about all the parables ([4:33–34](https://ref.ly/Mark4:33-Mark4:34)), not just this one.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`)

## Mark 4:11–12 (id: 21470)

* The true followers of Jesus could understand the secret of the kingdom of God. They knew the kingdom had arrived ([1:14–15](https://ref.ly/Mark1:14-Mark1:15)). They knew that the Christ, the Son of God, was among them ([1:1](https://ref.ly/Mark1:1)). To *those on the outside,* it seemed like riddles.

* When Jesus answered the disciples’ question, he showed that he used parables to keep outsiders from understanding. This was so that they would be unable to repent and be forgiven.

    The quotation from [Isaiah 6:9–10](https://ref.ly/Isa6:9-Isa6:10) in the Greek Old Testament version implies this was a deliberate judgment on the hearers. Jesus taught in parables, and outsiders did not believe. This fulfilled what the Scriptures had said would happen.

    The teaching of Jesus about the Kingdom of God was sensitive (it could sound like a political threat and worry Roman rulers). Because of this, teaching in parables helped make his message less likely to cause conflict.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Forgive (ID: `keyterm:Forgive`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 4:13 (id: 21471)

If people who heard Jesus could not understand the parable, they might not receive the gift of belonging to God’s Kingdom. But even his own disciples needed Jesus to explain the parables.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`)

## Mark 4:14–20 (id: 21472)

The explanation of the parable of the four soils uses an allegory (a story where each part has a deeper meaning). The *farmer* represents someone who shares God’s word ([4:14](https://ref.ly/Mark4:14)). The four types of *soil* represent different ways people respond to that message.

The main point of the parable is that people should be like *good soil* and listen to God’s word ([4:9](https://ref.ly/Mark4:9)). Only those who *produce a crop* (show a changed life) and continue faithfully to the end will be saved ([13:13](https://ref.ly/Mark13:13); [8:35](https://ref.ly/Mark8:35); [Revelation 2:10–11](https://ref.ly/Rev2:10-Rev2:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:14-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Always (ID: `keyterm:Always`); Cause of Joy (ID: `keyterm:CauseOfJoy`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Acacia (ID: `flora:Acacia`); Boxthorn (ID: `flora:Boxthorn`); Thistle (ID: `flora:Thistle`); Brier (ID: `flora:Brier`)

## Mark 4:21 (id: 21475)

*A lamp* is lit to shine and provide light, not to stay hidden. Citizens of the kingdom of God must display the light of the good news about Jesus ([4:17](https://ref.ly/Mark4:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`)

## Mark 4:21–25 (id: 21474)

These sayings connect with the call to listen carefully ([4:9](https://ref.ly/Mark4:9), [23–24](https://ref.ly/Mark4:23-Mark4:24)) and with the difference between insiders and outsiders ([4:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark4:11-Mark4:12), [24–25](https://ref.ly/Mark4:24-Mark4:25)). They show how people in God’s kingdom will live.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:21-25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Lampstand (ID: `realia:Lampstand.2`)

## Mark 4:22–23 (id: 21476)

One day, the lamp (that is, Jesus; compare [John 8:12](https://ref.ly/John8:12)) will reveal everything hidden. This includes every thought and secret action ([Mark 13:26](https://ref.ly/Mark13:26); [14:62](https://ref.ly/Mark14:62); see [Philippians 2:10–11](https://ref.ly/Phil2:10-Phil2:11)). The time to listen is now, before this happens ([Mark 4:9](https://ref.ly/Mark4:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:22-23


## Mark 4:24 (id: 21477)

Those who listen and respond will gain more understanding. But "those on the outside" ([4:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark4:11-Mark4:12)) will experience greater darkness ([2 Corinthians 3:14–16](https://ref.ly/2Cor3:14-2Cor3:16)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:24


## Mark 4:26–29 (id: 21478)

* This parable focuses on the seed and how it grows over time.
* Just as the earth produces crops on its own, the completion (final coming) of God’s kingdom does not depend on human effort.
* *first the stalk, then the head, then the grain that ripens within*: These describe the normal stages of a seed’s growth.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:26-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Sickle (ID: `realia:Sickle`)

## Mark 4:26–34 (id: 21479)

Mark now gives additional seed parables (see [4:3–20](https://ref.ly/Mark4:3-Mark4:20)). They all focus on "the mystery of the kingdom of God" ([4:11](https://ref.ly/Mark4:11)). The parables of the seed growing secretly ([4:26–29](https://ref.ly/Mark4:26-Mark4:29)) and the mustard seed ([4:30–32](https://ref.ly/Mark4:30-Mark4:32)) show different stages of seed growth. 

The kingdom of God is hidden ([4:21](https://ref.ly/Mark4:21)). But growth is certain ([4:26–29](https://ref.ly/Mark4:26-Mark4:29)). The outcome will be great ([4:32](https://ref.ly/Mark4:32)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:26-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Mustard (ID: `flora:Mustard`); Wheat (ID: `flora:Wheat`); Sickle (ID: `realia:Sickle`)

## Mark 4:29 (id: 21480)

Harvest time is like the final coming of God’s kingdom. A *sickle* often represents the final judgment ([Jeremiah 50:16](https://ref.ly/Jer50:16); [Joel 3:13](https://ref.ly/Joel3:13); [Revelation 14:14–19](https://ref.ly/Rev14:14-Rev14:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sickle (ID: `realia:Sickle`)

## Mark 4:30–32 (id: 21483)

Jesus contrasts a very small beginning with a large result. The mustard seed was known to be very small, almost the smallest seed people commonly used (see [Matthew 17:20](https://ref.ly/Matt17:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:30-32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Mustard (ID: `flora:Mustard`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Grain (ID: `flora:Grain`); Grass (ID: `flora:Grass`)

## Mark 4:33–34 (id: 21484)

* Mark’s summary of this section shows that [4:3–32](https://ref.ly/Mark4:3-Mark4:32) is a collection of parables. It is not a record of what Jesus taught on one specific day in the order in which he taught it.
* Jesus privately explained the parables to the disciples. This shows that they were not outsiders, even though they sometimes did not understand (compare [4:13](https://ref.ly/Mark4:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:33-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Mark 4:35–41 (id: 21485)

* This passage focuses on the greatness of Jesus Christ, the Son of God. The authority of Jesus over the forces of nature showed that he is divine (truly God).
* *the other side*: The eastern side of the Sea of Galilee was the region of the Gerasenes ([5:1](https://ref.ly/Mark5:1)).
* The presence of other boats following him shows how popular Jesus had become.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:35-41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Become Still (ID: `keyterm:BecomeStill`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Cushion (ID: `realia:Cushion`)

## Mark 4:35–5.43 (id: 21486)

The three miracle stories in this section are connected by:

* a shared location (the Sea of Galilee),
* the presence of the disciples,
* the use of a boat, and
* a common theme, *"Who is this"* ([4:41](https://ref.ly/Mark4:41)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:35-5:43

## Mark 4:38 (id: 21487)

Jesus was likely asleep at the back of the boat, using a sandbag as a pillow. In 1986, archaeologists found a boat from the time of Jesus in the mud near the northwest shore of the Sea of Galilee. It is 8 meters long (26\.5 feet), 2\.2 meters wide (7\.5 feet), and 1\.37 meters deep (4\.5 feet). It has a raised stern. It could hold up to 15 people.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cushion (ID: `realia:Cushion`)

## Mark 4:39–40 (id: 21488)

The disciples still lacked faith in Jesus ([4:41](https://ref.ly/Mark4:41)), even though they had many chances to experience his power and authority ([1:21–34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21-Mark1:34), [40–45](https://ref.ly/Mark1:40-Mark1:45); [2:1–12](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1-Mark2:12); [3:1–5](https://ref.ly/Mark3:1-Mark3:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:39-40

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Become Still (ID: `keyterm:BecomeStill`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Mark 4:41 (id: 21489)

* The disciples were very afraid. This is a common response to Jesus in Mark ([5:15](https://ref.ly/Mark5:15), [33](https://ref.ly/Mark5:33); [6:50](https://ref.ly/Mark6:50); [9:32](https://ref.ly/Mark9:32); [10:32](https://ref.ly/Mark10:32); [11:18](https://ref.ly/Mark11:18); [16:8](https://ref.ly/Mark16:8)).
* *Who is this*: Mark's audience already knows that Jesus is the Christ, the Son of God ([1:1](https://ref.ly/Mark1:1), [11](https://ref.ly/Mark1:11), [24](https://ref.ly/Mark1:24); [3:11](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 4:41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`)

## Mark 5:1 (id: 21496)

*in the region of the Gerasenes*: The exact location is uncertain because of differences in the manuscripts and geography. 

Manuscript evidence supports *Gerasa*, a city in this region located 59\.5 kilometers (37 miles) southeast of the Sea of Galilee ([5:13](https://ref.ly/Mark5:13)). 

Some manuscripts read *Gadarenes*. Gadara was eight kilometers (five miles) southeast of the Sea of Galilee. 

A few manuscripts read *Gergesenes*. The town of Gergesa was on a steep bank on the Sea of Galilee’s eastern shore.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gerasa (ID: `place:Gerasa`)

## Mark 5:1–20 (id: 21490)

Jesus and the disciples reached the other side of the lake, finishing the journey started in [4:35](https://ref.ly/Mark4:35). As in [1:21–28](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21-Mark1:28) and [3:11](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11), the demons recognized that Jesus was the Son of the Most High God ([1:34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:34)). For the first time in the Gospel of Mark, a Gentile was healed by Jesus (see study note on 5:10–13; compare [7:24–30](https://ref.ly/Mark7:24-Mark7:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:1-20
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 5:10-13 (ID: `21491@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

## Mark 5:2–5 (id: 21497)

Mark focuses on Jesus. The evil spirit was strong, but Jesus had even greater authority ([5:6–13](https://ref.ly/Mark5:6-Mark5:13)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:2-5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Grave (ID: `realia:Grave`); Chain (ID: `realia:Chain`); Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Fetter (ID: `realia:Fetter`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Mark 5:6–8 (id: 21513)

* Despite the man's terrible condition, the arrival of Jesus gave him hope. He ran and knelt before Jesus, asking for help.
* *Son of the Most High God*: Evil spirits know who Jesus really is ([1:24](https://ref.ly/Mark1:24), [34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:34); [3:11](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11)).
* There was no struggle. Jesus was in control, and the *unclean spirit* obeyed him. Torture is the final judgment awaiting unclean spirits ([Matthew 8:29](https://ref.ly/Matt8:29); [Luke 8:31](https://ref.ly/Luke8:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:6-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Bow Down (ID: `keyterm:BowDown`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 5:9 (id: 21514)

A *legion* was a Roman military unit of 5,000–6,000 men. Here it describes the presence of many evil spirits.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:9

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Legion (ID: `deity:Legion`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Mark 5:10–13 (id: 21491)

The spirits kept begging Jesus, which shows his authority over them. Jesus allowed them to enter a herd of about 2,000 pigs. The pigs show that this was Gentile (non\-Jewish) territory.

Some people focus on the loss of the pigs or ask why Jesus allowed the demons to destroy them. But Mark and his audience focused on how Jesus saved the man.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:10-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Mark 5:15–16 (id: 21492)

The change in the man shows the power Jesus has to save. Before, the man had been controlled by evil spirits. Now he was restored.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:15-16

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Demon Possessed (ID: `keyterm:DemonPossessed`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Pig (ID: `fauna:Pig`); Jackal (ID: `fauna:Jackal`)

## Mark 5:17 (id: 21493)

The frightened people asked Jesus to go away. Fear and amazement are frequent responses to the mighty acts of Jesus (see [1:22](https://ref.ly/Mark1:22), [27](https://ref.ly/Mark1:27); [2:12](https://ref.ly/Mark2:12); [4:41](https://ref.ly/Mark4:41); [6:50–51](https://ref.ly/Mark6:50-Mark6:51)). People reacted in different ways to the saving work of Jesus (see [4:14–20](https://ref.ly/Mark4:14-Mark4:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Mark 5:18 (id: 21494)

Unlike his neighbors, the man experienced God's grace and wanted *to go with Him* (literally *that he might be with him*; see [3:14](https://ref.ly/Mark3:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Be Demon Possessed (ID: `keyterm:DemonPossessed`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Mark 5:19–20 (id: 21495)

* Jesus wanted the man to tell others about the great things *the Lord* had done for him. Thus, Mark identifies Jesus as *the Lord*.
* *tell them how much*: Unlike other times, Jesus did not hide his identity, possibly because it was safer in this non\-Jewish area (see [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12)).
* *the Decapolis*: See study note on Matthew 4:24–25.

## Mark 5:21–22 (id: 21498)

*When Jesus had again crossed by boat to the other side* *…* *A synagogue leader named Jairus* *arrived*: Jairus organized the worship services at the synagogue and represented the Jewish community to the outside world (compare [Acts 13:15](https://ref.ly/Acts13:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jairus (ID: `person:Jairus`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Mark 5:21–43 (id: 21499)

Two healing miracles are connected by the need for faith ([5:34](https://ref.ly/Mark5:34), [36](https://ref.ly/Mark5:36)). The story of Jairus’s daughter begins, then the account of the woman with constant bleeding comes in the middle ([5:25–34](https://ref.ly/Mark5:25-Mark5:34)), and then the story of Jairus’s daughter continues. This pattern places the two stories together and connects them (see Mark Book Introduction, “Literary Features”). During the delay caused by the woman’s healing, Jairus’s daughter died ([5:35–43](https://ref.ly/Mark5:35-Mark5:43)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:21-43

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Jairus (ID: `person:Jairus`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); James (ID: `person:James`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Mark 5:23 (id: 21500)

The father wanted Jesus to heal her physically (Greek *so that she might be saved*; see [3:4](https://ref.ly/Mark3:4); [5:28](https://ref.ly/Mark5:28), [34](https://ref.ly/Mark5:34); [6:56](https://ref.ly/Mark6:56); [10:52](https://ref.ly/Mark10:52); [15:30–31](https://ref.ly/Mark15:30-Mark15:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Mark 5:24–34 (id: 21501)

As Jesus heads to Jairus's home, followed by a large crowd, Mark introduces a woman in the crowd who suffers from constant bleeding. This condition not only harms her health but also makes her ceremonially unclean. This prevents her from participating in normal community life (see [Leviticus 15:25–27](https://ref.ly/Lev15:25-Lev15:27)). She is an example of the faith that Jairus should have.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:24-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Blood (ID: `keyterm:Blood`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`)

## Mark 5:27–29 (id: 21502)

The woman's faith led her to believe that touching the clothing that Jesus was wearing would heal her, and it happened right away ([5:34](https://ref.ly/Mark5:34)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:27-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Cloak (ID: `realia:Cloak`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Body (ID: `keyterm:Body.3`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`)

## Mark 5:33–34 (id: 21503)

The woman felt afraid, not because she was guilty, but because she realized she had witnessed a powerful miracle from God. Unlike the fear of the people in the region of the Gerasenes ([5:15–17](https://ref.ly/Mark5:15-Mark5:17)), her fear was positive and led to Jesus blessing her. She experienced God’s grace and salvation.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:33-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Make Peace (ID: `keyterm:Peace`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Truth (ID: `keyterm:Truth`)

## Mark 5:35–43 (id: 21504)

Since Jairus had left home, his daughter had died. Jesus reassured Jairus that, despite the death of his daughter and the seeming hopelessness of the situation, Jairus should not be afraid but have faith in Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:35-43
