*just believe*: Like the woman did ([5:28](https://ref.ly/Mark5:28)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:36

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Mark 5:37 (id: 21506)

Peter, James, and John became the closest followers of Jesus (see [3:16–17](https://ref.ly/Mark3:16-Mark3:17); [9:2](https://ref.ly/Mark9:2); [14:33](https://ref.ly/Mark14:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John.2`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); James (ID: `person:James`)

## Mark 5:38 (id: 21507)

*the commotion … weeping and wailing loudly*: These things the messengers' report and emphasized the hopelessness of the situation. Such displays of grief were common at funerals in the time of Jesus, where people often hired professionals to sadly cry (see [Matthew 9:23](https://ref.ly/Matt9:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 5:39 (id: 21508)

Sleep is a metaphor for death ([John 11:11](https://ref.ly/John11:11); [1 Thessalonians 4:13–15](https://ref.ly/1Thess4:13-1Thess4:15)). The child is not dead because Jesus will resurrect her. She is only *asleep* and will soon "wake up" and continue her life. The same Greek word translated here as a*sleep* refers to death in [Matthew 27:52](https://ref.ly/Matt27:52); [Acts 7:60](https://ref.ly/Acts7:60); [13:36](https://ref.ly/Acts13:36); [1 Corinthians 15:6](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:6), [18](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:18), [20](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:20), [51](https://ref.ly/1Cor15:51); [1 Thessalonians 4:13–15](https://ref.ly/1Thess4:13-1Thess4:15).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:39


## Mark 5:40 (id: 21509)

The crowd did not understand what Jesus meant. They laughed at him, knowing the girl had died. He made them all leave*,* probably to keep them from discouraging the parents any further.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:40


## Mark 5:41 (id: 21510)

Mark translates *Talitha koum* for his Greek\-speaking audience as *"Little girl, I say to you, get up!"* These Aramaic words are not magical. They are simply what Jesus said when he raised the girl. Aramaic was his native language. Mark likely learned the story in Aramaic while in Jerusalem.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:41


## Mark 5:42 (id: 21511)

Jesus showed his power over death when the little girl stood up and walked immediately. She did not need time to recover. The change was instant and complete.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:42


## Mark 5:43 (id: 21512)

* After the miracle, there is a command not to tell anyone about it (see [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12)).
* *give her something to eat*: The Lord, who controls creation, demons, illness, and death, also cares about the daily needs of his creatures.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 5:43


## Mark 6:1–2 (id: 21515)

* The teaching of Jesus amazed the people because of his wisdom and his ability to heal and drive out demons.
* *"Where did this man get"*: His early life in Nazareth had not prepared them to accept him as a teacher and healer who spoke and acted with authority.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:1-2

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Mark 6:1–6 (id: 21516)

Nazareth’s rejection of Jesus stands in sharp contrast to the faith shown by others. The few miracles he did there also contrast with the greater displays of power seen in other places.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:1-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.3`); Jude (ID: `person:Jude`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.3`); Disbelieve (ID: `keyterm:Disbelieve`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Sabbath (ID: `keyterm:Sabbath`); James (ID: `person:James.3`); Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`); Synagogue (ID: `realia:Synagogue`)

## Mark 6:3–4 (id: 21528)

* The people of Nazareth saw Jesus only as a carpenter (literally *craftsman*).
* *He is just a carpenter, the son of Mary*: Calling Jesus *the son of Mary* instead of the son of Joseph likely means Joseph had died by then.
* *brother … his sisters*: See study note on 3:31–35.
* *They were very offended*: They believed Jesus was pretending to be someone he could not possibly be.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:3-4
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 3:31-35 (ID: `21462@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Joseph (ID: `person:Joseph.3`); Jude (ID: `person:Jude`); Simon (ID: `person:Simon.3`); Mary (ID: `person:Mary`); James (ID: `person:James.3`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Mark 6:5–6 (id: 21541)

* Faith and healing are often connected in Mark ([2:5](https://ref.ly/Mark2:5); [5:34](https://ref.ly/Mark5:34), [36](https://ref.ly/Mark5:36); [9:23–24](https://ref.ly/Mark9:23-Mark9:24); [10:52](https://ref.ly/Mark10:52)). The miracles of Jesus were not performances but miraculous signs of God's kingdom. To enter this kingdom and receive its benefits, one must repent and have faith ([1:15](https://ref.ly/Mark1:15)).
* Jesus healed some sick people, but the lack of faith in his own village amazed Jesus. This was unusual.
* This part of Mark describes Jesus being rejected by ([3:7–6:6a](https://ref.ly/Mark3:7-Mark6:6)):

    + his family ([3:21](https://ref.ly/Mark3:21), [31–35](https://ref.ly/Mark3:31-Mark3:35)),
        + the scribes ([3:22](https://ref.ly/Mark3:22)), and
        + the people of Gerasa ([5:17](https://ref.ly/Mark5:17)).
        It ends with rejection by people in his hometown ([6:1–6a](https://ref.ly/Mark6:1-Mark6:6)).

    Along the way, a few people believed and Jesus healed them. This ending is similar to the end of the previous section ([1:14–3:6](https://ref.ly/Mark1:14-Mark3:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disbelieve (ID: `keyterm:Disbelieve`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Serve (ID: `keyterm:Serve`)

## Mark 6:6 (id: 21548)

This summary of the healing and preaching ministry of Jesus leads to a story about the disciples. Jesus likely taught from village to village near the Sea of Galilee, and perhaps in Capernaum (see [2:1](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disbelieve (ID: `keyterm:Disbelieve`)

## Mark 6:6–8.21 (id: 21547)

The mission of Jesus and the misunderstanding of the disciples are prominent themes in this section.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:6-13

## Mark 6:7 (id: 21550)

* *two by two*: This practice fulfilled the Old Testament rule of needing two witnesses ([Numbers 35:30](https://ref.ly/Num35:30); [Deuteronomy 17:6](https://ref.ly/Deut17:6); [19:15](https://ref.ly/Deut19:15); see [Matthew 18:16](https://ref.ly/Matt18:16)). It also allowed for them to have companionship and mutual help [Acts 13:1–3](https://ref.ly/Acts13:1-Acts13:3); [15:22](https://ref.ly/Acts15:22), [39](https://ref.ly/Acts15:39), [40](https://ref.ly/Acts15:40)).
* *giving them authority*: Jesus can give his authority to others (see [Mark 1:27](https://ref.ly/Mark1:27); [2:10](https://ref.ly/Mark2:10)).
* Jesus told the disciples to drive out *unclean spirits* and to preach and heal ([6:12–13](https://ref.ly/Mark6:12-Mark6:13); see [6:30](https://ref.ly/Mark6:30)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Authority (ID: `keyterm:Authority.2`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Mark 6:7–13 (id: 21549)

Jesus sent the disciples to preach and heal. During this mission, they memorized the teachings of Jesus by repeating them. This prepared them to later recall and share what Jesus did and taught ([Luke 1:2](https://ref.ly/Luke1:2)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:7-13

## Mark 6:8–9 (id: 21551)

The instructions for the disciples' mission focused on what they should leave behind (*bread …bag …money* … *second tunic*). They were to travel with little and rely on Jewish kindness to strangers for food and a place to sleep at night (see [Luke 22:35–37](https://ref.ly/Luke22:35-Luke22:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:8-9

## Mark 6:10 (id: 21517)

They were to sleep in the same house and not take advantage of kindness of their hosts by looking for better food and another place to sleep at night.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 6:11 (id: 21518)

*shake the dust off your feet*: This represents God's judgment on those who rejected the apostles' message (see [Acts 18:6](https://ref.ly/Acts18:6)). To reject it was actually to reject Jesus and God, who sent him ([9:37](https://ref.ly/Mark9:37)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bear Witness (ID: `keyterm:BearWitness`)

## Mark 6:12–13 (id: 21519)

The call to repent is a short form of the fuller message in [1:15](https://ref.ly/Mark1:15).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:12-13

## Mark 6:14 (id: 21521)

* *Jesus’ name had become well known*: The successful mission in Galilee and Perea by six teams of disciples helped spread news about him throughout the region ([6:12–13](https://ref.ly/Mark6:12-Mark6:13)).
* *John the Baptist has risen from the dead!*: King Herod may have thought that John the Baptist had come back to life and was now working through Jesus. This idea is similar to how Elijah's spirit came upon Elisha.

    Or, he might have been expressing frustration, saying something like, “This is John the Baptist all over again!” (the same kind of powerful prophet has appeared again).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Resurrection (ID: `keyterm:Resurrection`); Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); John (ID: `person:John`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Mark 6:14–29 (id: 21520)

The story of John the Baptist's death, placed between the disciples' mission and return, emphasizes the authority and power of Jesus (see [6:7](https://ref.ly/Mark6:7), [14](https://ref.ly/Mark6:14)). John's end and the warning to the disciples during their mission also point ahead to the death of Jesus ([6:11](https://ref.ly/Mark6:11)). The one greater even than John ([1:8](https://ref.ly/Mark1:8)) would soon be handed over to religious and political leaders and put to death ([8:31](https://ref.ly/Mark8:31); [9:31](https://ref.ly/Mark9:31); [10:33](https://ref.ly/Mark10:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:14-29

The return of the prophet Elijah had been prophesied in [Malachi 3:1](https://ref.ly/Mal3:1) and [4:5–6](https://ref.ly/Mal4:5-Mal4:6) (see also [Mark 9:11–13](https://ref.ly/Mark9:11-Mark9:13)).

## Mark 6:17 (id: 21523)

* *that John be arrested and bound and imprisoned*: The Jewish historian Josephus (*Antiquities* 18\.5\.2\) says that John was imprisoned in the fortress of Machaerus in Perea.

* Josephus (*Antiquities* 18\.5\.1–4\) also mentions that Herodias was the wife of Herod Antipas's half\-brother, Herod Philip.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:17

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Philip (ID: `person:Philip.2`); Herodias (ID: `person:Herodias`); John (ID: `person:John`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

## Mark 6:18 (id: 21524)

* Mark explains the moral and religious reason for the arrest and execution of John the Baptist. The Jewish historian Josephus gives a political reason instead. He says Herod feared that John’s popularity might lead to a rebellion (Josephus, *Antiquities* 18\.5\.2\).

    In a similar way, the charges against Jesus began as religious concerns ([Mark 14:63–64](https://ref.ly/Mark14:63-Mark14:64)). But when he was brought before Pontius Pilate, the charges became political ([15:2](https://ref.ly/Mark15:2); [Luke 23:2–3](https://ref.ly/Luke23:2-Luke23:3)).

* *It is not lawful*: John said that Herod’s marriage was wrong. It was adultery ([Leviticus 20:10](https://ref.ly/Lev20:10)). It also broke the law that forbids a man from marrying his brother’s wife ([Leviticus 18:16](https://ref.ly/Lev18:16); [20:21](https://ref.ly/Lev20:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Lawful (ID: `keyterm:Lawful`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); John (ID: `person:John`)

## Mark 6:19–28 (id: 21525)

Herodias's role in John the Baptist's murder is similar to the story of Jezebel ([1 Kings 19:1–2](https://ref.ly/1Kgs19:1-1Kgs19:2)). Like Jezebel, she pushed for the death of a prophet.

Herod gave in to pressure ([Mark 6:26–28](https://ref.ly/Mark6:26-Mark6:28)). This points ahead to what Pontius Pilate later did. Pilate ordered that Jesus be put to death even though he believed Jesus was innocent (compare [6:20](https://ref.ly/Mark6:20), [25–28](https://ref.ly/Mark6:25-Mark6:28) with [15:6–15](https://ref.ly/Mark15:6-Mark15:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:19-28

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** John (ID: `person:John`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Herodias (ID: `person:Herodias`); Plate (ID: `realia:Plate.2`); Daughter of Herodias (ID: `person:DaughterOfHerodias`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Galilee (ID: `place:Galilee`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Be (Or Show Oneself) Just (ID: `keyterm:Just`); Oath (ID: `keyterm:Oath`); Haste (ID: `keyterm:Haste`); Prison (ID: `realia:Prison`)

*the daughter of Herodias*: The first\-century Jewish historian Josephus wrote that Herodias's daughter was also named Salome (Josephus, *Antiquities* 18\.5\.4\).

This verse is similar to the description of the death and burial of Jesus (see [15:43–46](https://ref.ly/Mark15:43-Mark15:46)). Disciples of John existed after his death until at least the AD 200s (see [Acts 18:24–19:7](https://ref.ly/Acts18:24-Acts19:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:29

## Mark 6:30 (id: 21530)

* Mark refers to the disciples of Jesus as *apostles* to show their new role as those who had been sent out (see study note on 3:14–15). This also helps avoid confusion with John the Baptist’s disciples.
* The apostles reported the success of what they had done and taught.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:30

The disciples finish their mission ([6:6–13](https://ref.ly/Mark6:6-Mark6:13), [30–34](https://ref.ly/Mark6:30-Mark6:34)). Then the story of feeding the 5,000 follows ([6:35–44](https://ref.ly/Mark6:35-Mark6:44); see also [Matthew 14:13–21](https://ref.ly/Matt14:13-Matt14:21); [Luke 9:10–17](https://ref.ly/Luke9:10-Luke9:17); [John 6:1–15](https://ref.ly/John6:1-John6:15)). The feeding of the 4,000 is different enough to show these were two separate events (see [Mark 8:1–10](https://ref.ly/Mark8:1-Mark8:10)).

## Mark 6:31–33 (id: 21531)

* *Come with me*: Jesus and the disciples likely traveled north along the western shore of the Sea of Galilee toward Bethsaida ([Luke 9:10](https://ref.ly/Luke9:10)). As they moved, the crowds could still see them along the shore.
* *many people*: The large crowds show the success of the apostles' mission and the greatness of Jesus, whom the apostles represented.

## Mark 6:34 (id: 21532)

*they were like sheep without a shepherd:* God's people need guidance ([Numbers 27:16–17](https://ref.ly/Num27:16-Num27:17); [1 Kings 22:17](https://ref.ly/1Kgs22:17); [2 Chronicles 18:16](https://ref.ly/2Chr18:16); see [Jeremiah 13:10](https://ref.ly/Jer13:10); [Ezekiel 34:23](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:23)). In Jewish writings, teaching God's word is like feeding sheep (for example, [Ezekiel 34:1–24](https://ref.ly/Ezek34:1-Ezek34:24); *2 Baruch* 77:13–15\).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:34

The place where Jesus taught was remote (far from towns), but it still had farms and villages nearby where people could buy food.

## Mark 6:37 (id: 21534)

Jesus suggests that, just as the disciples were able to drive out demons and heal through his power ([6:7](https://ref.ly/Mark6:7), [13](https://ref.ly/Mark6:13), [30](https://ref.ly/Mark6:30)), they should be able to feed the crowd. Their reply shows that they were thinking only about what they could do on their own. They did not take into account the power of Jesus (his ability to act with God’s authority).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Denarius (ID: `realia:Denarius`)

## Mark 6:41 (id: 21535)

The words *taking*, *blessed*, *broke*, and *gave* reflect the words Jesus spoke at the Last Supper ([14:22–25](https://ref.ly/Mark14:22-Mark14:25)). Both events imagine the final messianic feast where Christians will eat and drink with Jesus in the kingdom of God ([14:25](https://ref.ly/Mark14:25); [Matthew 5:6](https://ref.ly/Matt5:6); [22:1–10](https://ref.ly/Matt22:1-Matt22:10); see [Revelation 2:7](https://ref.ly/Rev2:7); [19:7–9](https://ref.ly/Rev19:7-Rev19:9); [22:1–2](https://ref.ly/Rev22:1-Rev22:2), [14](https://ref.ly/Rev22:14), [17–19](https://ref.ly/Rev22:17-Rev22:19)).

This miracle makes Elisha’s miracle of feeding 100 people with 20 loaves seem less impressive ([2 Kings 4:42–44](https://ref.ly/2Kgs4:42-2Kgs4:44)). We should ask ourselves, “Who is this?” ([Mark 4:41](https://ref.ly/Mark4:41)). He is the Christ, the Son of God ([1:1](https://ref.ly/Mark1:1)).

## Mark 6:45–46 (id: 21537)

The disciples went to Bethsaida, the hometown of Peter and Andrew ([John 1:44](https://ref.ly/John1:44)). Meanwhile, Jesus dismissed the crowd and went away by himself to pray.

Jesus regularly prayed ([Mark 1:35](https://ref.ly/Mark1:35)). He also urged his disciples to pray (see [14:38](https://ref.ly/Mark14:38)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:45-46

## Mark 6:45–52 (id: 21538)

This story shows the glory of Jesus (his divine power and greatness) to the disciples ([6:48–50](https://ref.ly/Mark6:48-Mark6:50)). It is also a rescue story, because Jesus comes to help them in danger ([6:47–48](https://ref.ly/Mark6:47-Mark6:48), [51](https://ref.ly/Mark6:51)). 

At the same time, it shows that the disciples did not understand what was happening ([6:51–52](https://ref.ly/Mark6:51-Mark6:52)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:45-52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); Bethsaida (ID: `place:Bethsaida`); Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Cheer Up (ID: `keyterm:CheerUp`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Mark 6:48 (id: 21539)

* *About the fourth watch of the night*: This was about three o'clock in the morning. This Roman way of telling time suggests that Mark wrote for Christians in Rome.
* Mark does not explain how Jesus saw his disciples struggling in the middle of the lake at night. It is not important to the story.
* *Jesus went out to them, walking on the sea*: It seems Jesus came to rescue them from the storm. But Mark says that Jesus *intended to pass by them*. Many have tried to explain this, but perhaps the best explanation is that *to pass them* *by* refers to a divine appearance. This is similar to events in Exodus and 1 Kings, where divine glory is shown. The disciples' fear supports this idea, as fear often accompanies divine appearances.
* Many have tried to explain the miracle of Jesus walking on water. But Mark, Matthew, and John saw it as a miracle without a natural explanation. If Jesus is truly the Son of God, there needs to be no other explanation.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:48


## Mark 6:49–50 (id: 21540)

* The disciples were very afraid, and this reaction makes sense. People cannot walk on water, so they thought they were seeing a ghost.

    As in many times when God appears (divine manifestations, when God shows himself), the Lord spoke words of comfort: *"**Take courage! It is I. Do not be afraid."*

    It was not a ghost but Jesus (their friend, Savior, and Lord).

* *It is I* : Jesus walking on the sea and calming the storm were miracles (acts of God’s power). Because of this, Mark’s first audience would have understood the words *I am* as more than a simple statement. They would have heard a connection to how God describes himself in the Old Testament ([Exodus 3:14](https://ref.ly/Exod3:14); [Deuteronomy 32:39](https://ref.ly/Deut32:39); [Isaiah 41:4](https://ref.ly/Isa41:4); [43:10–13](https://ref.ly/Isa43:10-Isa43:13)). This suggests that Jesus shares in God’s identity (he is divine).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:49-50

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Cheer Up (ID: `keyterm:CheerUp`)

## Mark 6:51 (id: 21542)

* When Jesus got into the boat, the storm stopped.
* *utterly astounded*: The disciples were filled with awe and reverence (deep respect and wonder) at the greatness of Jesus Christ, the Son of God (compare [2:12](https://ref.ly/Mark2:12); [5:24](https://ref.ly/Mark5:24); [Matthew 14:33](https://ref.ly/Matt14:33)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:51

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Mark 6:52 (id: 21543)

The account ends with a comment about the disciples’ lack of understanding. This was not because they lacked the opportunity to believe in Jesus. They had seen the miracle of the loaves and many other miracles.

Instead, their hearts were too hard (they were not open to understanding). This is usually a quality of those who oppose Jesus (for example, [3:5](https://ref.ly/Mark3:5); see also [8:17](https://ref.ly/Mark8:17), [21](https://ref.ly/Mark8:21); [9:32](https://ref.ly/Mark9:32)). 

Later, their hearts would be changed. They would come to understand these things ([Luke 24:44–49](https://ref.ly/Luke24:44-Luke24:49); [John 12:16](https://ref.ly/John12:16); [13:7](https://ref.ly/John13:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:52

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Mark 6:53 (id: 21545)

Gennesaret was a fertile plain (good land for growing crops), about 5\.6 kilometers long (3\.5 miles) and 0\.8 kilometers wide (0\.5 miles). It lay between Tiberias and Capernaum on the northwest shore of the Sea of Galilee.

The disciples first planned to go to Bethsaida ([6:45](https://ref.ly/Mark6:45)). One possibility is that strong winds ([6:](https://ref.ly/Mark6:45)[48](https://ref.ly/Mark6:48)) pushed the boat off course, and they landed at Gennesaret instead. Another possibility is that [6:45](https://ref.ly/Mark6:45) describes the direction of travel (toward the northeast), not the exact destination.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:53

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Gennesaret (ID: `place:Gennesaret`)

## Mark 6:53–56 (id: 21544)

This short summary of the work Jesus did in Galilee shows how popular he was and how he healed many people.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 6:53-56

## Mark 6:56 (id: 21546)

*the fringe of His cloak*: This refers to the tassels that Jewish men wore on the edges of their robes (see [Numbers 15:38–39](https://ref.ly/Num15:38-Num15:39); [Deuteronomy 22:12](https://ref.ly/Deut22:12)). Touching the *fringe of His cloak* brought healing to sick people (see also [Mark 3:10](https://ref.ly/Mark3:10); [5:28](https://ref.ly/Mark5:28)).

## Mark 7:1 (id: 21556)

Mark's audience already knows that the Pharisees and teachers of religious law were hostile to Jesus (see [2:16](https://ref.ly/Mark2:16), [24](https://ref.ly/Mark2:24); [3:6](https://ref.ly/Mark3:6), [22](https://ref.ly/Mark3:22)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`)

## Mark 7:1–23 (id: 21552)

This account does not connect directly to what comes before it. It simply fits a general setting, such as "At one point in the ministry of Jesus." 

After Mark sets the scene ([7:1–4](https://ref.ly/Mark7:1-Mark7:4)), he introduces the Pharisees' question ([7:](https://ref.ly/Mark7:6-Mark7:23)[5](https://ref.ly/Mark7:5)). Then Jesus responds to the question ([6–23](https://ref.ly/Mark7:6-Mark7:23)). 

The first part of the response focuses on two Old Testament passages ([7:6–13](https://ref.ly/Mark7:6-Mark7:13)). It also includes a two\-part critique (strong criticism) of the Pharisees’ traditions ([7:6–8](https://ref.ly/Mark7:6-Mark7:8), [9–13](https://ref.ly/Mark7:9-Mark7:13)). 

In the second part, Jesus explains what truly defiles a person (what makes someone unclean before God; [7:14–23](https://ref.ly/Mark7:14-Mark7:23)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:1-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Teaching (ID: `keyterm:Teaching`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Temple Treasury (ID: `place:Corban`); Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`); Live (ID: `keyterm:Live.3`); Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Jerusalem (ID: `place:Jerusalem`); Cooking Pot (ID: `realia:CookingPot`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Mark 7:2–4 (id: 21557)

Mark explains the Pharisees’ practices of ritual cleansing (ceremonial washing for religious purity) for his non\-Jewish (Gentile) audience.

The *traditions of the elders* were not yet written down at that time. The Pharisees believed these teachings had been given to Moses on Mount Sinai along with the written law.

These traditions were later written down around AD 200 in the *Mishnah* (a collection of Jewish teachings and laws). The Mishnah is one of the main sources for understanding ancient Judaism.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:2-4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Jews (ID: `group:Jews`); Baptism (ID: `keyterm:Baptism`); Teaching (ID: `keyterm:Teaching`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Cooking Pot (ID: `realia:CookingPot`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Mark 7:5 (id: 21568)

Since the Pharisees believed that their oral traditions were given to Moses as part of God’s divine revelation, they asked Jesus why his disciples did not keep them. After all, teachers were responsible for the behavior of their disciples (see [2:24](https://ref.ly/Mark2:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Religious Officials (ID: `group:ReligiousOfficials`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Live (ID: `keyterm:Live.3`); Teaching (ID: `keyterm:Teaching`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Mark 7:6–8 (id: 21569)

Jesus first responds by quoting [Isaiah 29:13](https://ref.ly/Isa29:13) (from the Greek version). This passage speaks about the problem of treating human ideas as if they were God’s commands.

It describes well what was happening with the Pharisees. They had replaced God’s law with their own traditions (human rules and teachings).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:6-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Teaching (ID: `keyterm:Teaching`); Speak as a Prophet (ID: `keyterm:Speak`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Worship (ID: `keyterm:Worship`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`); Isaiah (ID: `person:Isaiah`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 7:9–13 (id: 21570)

Next, Jesus gives an example where the Pharisees’ traditions went against God’s law. Their traditions allowed them to avoid what God’s law required.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:9-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Teaching (ID: `keyterm:Teaching`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Temple Treasury (ID: `place:Corban`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Commandment (ID: `keyterm:Commandment`)

## Mark 7:11–12 (id: 21553)

* *But you say*: The contrast with God’s law is very strong. Their tradition went against God’s command to honor parents by caring for their needs.
* ‘*Whatever you would have received from me is Corban’ (that is, a gift devoted to God)*: This tradition taught that people could avoid helping their parents. They could promise to give their resources to God instead. In this way, they ignored and dishonored their parents, even when their parents were in need.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:11-12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple Treasury (ID: `place:Corban`); Offering (ID: `keyterm:Offering`)

## Mark 7:13 (id: 21554)

* The result of such traditions was to cancel the word of God.
* *in many such matters*: This was not a unique instance (see, for example, [Isaiah 1:10–20](https://ref.ly/Isa1:10-Isa1:20) and [58:1–14](https://ref.ly/Isa58:1-Isa58:14)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Teaching (ID: `keyterm:Teaching`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 7:14–23 (id: 21555)

The second argument of Jesus against the Pharisees was a proverb he told the crowd. Later, in a private setting, Jesus explained it to his disciples ([7:17–23](https://ref.ly/Mark7:17-Mark7:23)). It concerns moral distinctions about eating (rules about what is right or wrong to eat). Eating affects the system that processes food, but moral issues involve the heart. The heart is a metaphor for where a person makes moral decisions. 

How a person eats (with ceremonially clean or unclean hands) or what a person eats (ceremonially clean or unclean food) only affects the system that processes food. So it does not impact the moral issues of the human soul or inner being. To help his later audience understand the meaning of this teaching, Mark adds this comment *Thus all foods are clean*. 

In the early church, Peter and Paul helped make this meaning clear (see [Acts 10:1–11:18](https://ref.ly/Acts10:1-Acts11:18); [Romans 14:14](https://ref.ly/Rom14:14); [1 Corinthians 8:8](https://ref.ly/1Cor8:8); [Galatians 2:11–21](https://ref.ly/Gal2:11-Gal2:21)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:14-23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Parable (ID: `keyterm:Parable`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`); To Cleanse (ID: `keyterm:Cleanse.2`); Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Mark 7:20–22 (id: 21558)

These verses list 13 things that truly make a person unclean (morally impure). These actions and wrong desires come from the heart (a person’s inner thoughts and intentions).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:20-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Blaspheme (ID: `keyterm:Blaspheme`); Defilement (ID: `keyterm:Defilement`); Fornication (ID: `keyterm:Fornication`)

## Mark 7:24 (id: 21560)

* The city of Tyre and its nearby areas are on the Mediterranean coast in modern Lebanon. Tyre was originally an island but became a peninsula when Alexander the Great built a half\-mile ramp from the shore in 332 BC.

    Tyre had one of the most important harbors on the eastern Mediterranean Sea. People from Tyre had previously come to hear Jesus and see his miracles ([3:7–8](https://ref.ly/Mark3:7-Mark3:8)). This explains how the woman from Tyre knew about him.

* There is much discussion about why Jesus left Galilee to visit this Gentile (non\-Jewish) region. Mark does not explain the reason, so any suggestion is only a guess.

    However, Mark’s Gentile audience would have understood this journey as pointing ahead to the church’s mission to the Gentile world. This mission later led to their own coming to faith (for more about how the church began in Rome, see Romans Book Introduction).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 7:24–30 (id: 21559)

The discussion of clean and unclean and of undefiled and defiled in [7:1–23](https://ref.ly/Mark7:1-Mark7:23) has prepared Mark's audience for the story of the Gentile woman’s faith. Jews considered Gentiles to be defiled because they did not keep the regulations of the Torah (see [Acts 10:1–11:18](https://ref.ly/Acts10:1-Acts11:18), especially [10:14–15](https://ref.ly/Mark10:14-Mark10:15); [11:2–3](https://ref.ly/Mark11:2-Mark11:3), [8–9](https://ref.ly/Mark11:8-Mark11:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:24-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Dog (ID: `fauna:Dog`); Phoenicia (ID: `place:Phoenicia`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); Syrophoenicians (ID: `group:Phoenicia`); Greeks (ID: `group:Greek`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Honest (ID: `keyterm:Honest`); Javan (ID: `person:Javan`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`)

## Mark 7:25–26 (id: 21561)

The woman was from Syrian Phoenicia and therefore a Gentile. The urgency and intensity of the woman’s petition is seen in her falling at the feet of Jesus and begging.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:25-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Phoenicia (ID: `place:Phoenicia`); Syrophoenicians (ID: `group:Phoenicia`); Greeks (ID: `group:Greek`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Javan (ID: `person:Javan`)

## Mark 7:27–28 (id: 21562)

* Jesus responds to the woman in what might seem like a severe way. But their interaction shows that Jesus wanted to see her faith (compare [Matthew 15:28](https://ref.ly/Matt15:28)).
* Jesus did not use the word *first* to exclude her as a Gentile (non\-Jew) from God’s grace (kindness and favor). Instead, it showed that his mission was directed first to the Jews ([Acts 13:46](https://ref.ly/Acts13:46); [Romans 1:16](https://ref.ly/Rom1:16)).

    The woman replied with humility and faith. She acknowledged the priority of the Jewish people but argued that there was more than enough food for the dogs as well.

    Jewish writings often referred to Gentiles as dogs. Dogs were seen as dirty scavengers rather than family pets (see also study note on Revelation 22:15).

    The woman’s persistence and acknowledgment of Jesus as Lord were rewarded.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:27-28
* **Associated Articles:** Revelation 22:15 (ID: `25419@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Dog (ID: `fauna:Dog`); Honest (ID: `keyterm:Honest`)

## Mark 7:29–30 (id: 21563)

For similar healings from a distance, see [Matthew 8:5–13](https://ref.ly/Matt8:5-Matt8:13), [Luke 7:1–10](https://ref.ly/Luke7:1-Luke7:10), and [John 4:46–54](https://ref.ly/John4:46-John4:54).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:29-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); Bed (ID: `realia:Bed`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 7:31–37 (id: 21564)

* This miracle is very similar in order and wording to the healing of the blind man in [8:22–26](https://ref.ly/Mark8:22-Mark8:26). Healing miracles in the Gospels often follow a similar pattern. As these stories were told and retold, their form and wording likely became more consistent.
* This miracle also includes a change of location. Some think the next miracle happened in a Gentile area such as Sidon or the Decapolis. However, it is more likely that it took place after Jesus returned to the Sea of Galilee. The next event clearly happens there ([8:10](https://ref.ly/Mark8:10)), with no change in location mentioned.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:31-37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Decapolis (ID: `place:Decapolis`); Sidon (ID: `place:Sidon`); Sea of Galilee (ID: `place:GalileeSea`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Tyre (ID: `place:Tyre`); Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`)

## Mark 7:33 (id: 21565)

* The healing was done in private ([7:33](https://ref.ly/Mark7:33); see [5:40–43](https://ref.ly/Mark5:40-Mark5:43); [8:23](https://ref.ly/Mark8:23)). This likely helped prevent people from spreading strong excitement about Jesus as the Messiah (God’s chosen king; see [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12); compare [John 6:15](https://ref.ly/John6:15)).
* Jesus also used saliva in another healing ([Mark 8:23](https://ref.ly/Mark8:23)), when he put it on a man’s eyes to heal his blindness. The use of saliva for healing was common in the ancient world. People believed it could help heal certain conditions.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:33


## Mark 7:34 (id: 21566)

* *And looking up to heaven, He sighed deeply*:Since Jesus was looking up to heaven when he sighed*,* his sigh is probably best understood as a prayerful gesture.
* *Ephphatha* is an Aramaic word that Mark translates for his audience (see also [3:17](https://ref.ly/Mark3:17); [5:41](https://ref.ly/Mark5:41); [14:36](https://ref.ly/Mark14:36); [15:34](https://ref.ly/Mark15:34)). These are not magical words. Mark is simply sharing some of the original words Jesus spoke.

    Matthew and Luke do not seem to have attributed any special importance to the Aramaic words of Jesus, since they did not include them in their Gospels.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Mark 7:36–37 (id: 21567)

Even though Jesus tried to avoid attention, his greatness could not be hidden. His character, his teaching, and his power to heal filled people with awe (deep wonder and respect).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 7:36-37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Proclaim (ID: `keyterm:Proclaim`)

## Mark 8:1–3 (id: 21572)

The last mention of a large crowd was during the feeding of the 5,000 ([6:34](https://ref.ly/Mark6:34)). Jesus showed compassion in [6:34](https://ref.ly/Mark6:34) because the people were "like sheep without a shepherd." 

These stories show the concern Jesus has for the whole person. Here, he especially focuses on their hunger. They were in the wilderness and had no food ([8:1,](https://ref.ly/Mark8:1)[4](https://ref.ly/Mark8:4)). They had been with Jesus for three days without food and had traveled a long way ([8:2](https://ref.ly/Mark8:2)). They needed to eat before going home ([8:3](https://ref.ly/Mark8:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:1-3

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 8:1–10 (id: 21571)

The stories about feeding the 4,000 and the 5,000 are similar. All four Gospels report the earlier feeding of the 5,000 ([6:35–44](https://ref.ly/Mark6:35-Mark6:44); [Matthew 14:13–21](https://ref.ly/Matt14:13-Matt14:21); [Luke 9:10–17](https://ref.ly/Luke9:10-Luke9:17); [John 6:1–15](https://ref.ly/John6:1-John6:15)). Only Mark and Matthew also record the feeding of the 4,000 as a separate miracle ([Matthew 15:32–39](https://ref.ly/Matt15:32-Matt15:39); [Mark 8:19–21](https://ref.ly/Mark8:19-Mark8:21); [Matthew 16:9–10](https://ref.ly/Matt16:9-Matt16:10)). The details of the events are different.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:1-10

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Magadan (ID: `place:Magadan`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Basket (ID: `realia:Basket`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`)

## Mark 8:4 (id: 21599)

The disciples' question shows their lack of understanding. They knew God provided manna in the wilderness during the time of Moses ([Exodus 16](https://ref.ly/Exod16:1-Exod16:36)). They were now with someone much greater than Moses (see [Mark 9:5–7](https://ref.ly/Mark9:5-Mark9:7)). They had recently seen him feed more people in a similar situation ([6:30–44](https://ref.ly/Mark6:30-Mark6:44)). 

But they still did not understand (compare [6:52](https://ref.ly/Mark6:52)). Even so, Jesus used them to provide physical and spiritual food to the hungry ([8:6–8](https://ref.ly/Mark8:6-Mark8:8)). What they could not do alone, the powerful Son of God did through them.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`)

## Mark 8:5 (id: 21600)

*Seven*: Many people have tried to find symbolic meaning in the numbers in the two feeding miracles. However, there is no agreement among these interpretations. The mention of "a few fish" in this story also suggests that the numbers are not symbolic.

None of the Gospel writers explain any special meaning for these numbers. This should caution us not to assign meaning to them.

As with the feeding of the five thousand, the numbers mainly serve to highlight the greatness of the miracle and of Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:5

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`)

## Mark 8:6–7 (id: 21601)

*took … gave thanks … distributed*: These terms describe the actions of Jesus during this event. These same actions appear later the Last Supper (compare [14:22–25](https://ref.ly/Mark14:22-Mark14:25)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:6-7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Bless (ID: `keyterm:Bless`); Fish (ID: `fauna:Fish`)

## Mark 8:8 (id: 21602)

They ate as much as they wanted. Having plenty of food shows the greatness of God’s kingdom and the one who brought it.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Basket (ID: `realia:Basket`)

## Mark 8:10–13 (id: 21573)

* Like the earlier miracle where Jesus fed 5,000 people ([6:30–44](https://ref.ly/Mark6:30-Mark6:44)), after feeding 4,000 people, Jesus traveled across the Sea of Galilee by boat (compare [6:45](https://ref.ly/Mark6:45)). When he arrived, the Pharisees came to argue with him. They demanded that Jesus show them a miracle as proof (compare [7:1–23](https://ref.ly/Mark7:1-Mark7:23)).
* We do not know where Dalmanutha was located. [Matthew 15:39](https://ref.ly/Matt15:39) uses the name *Magadan* instead of *Dalmanutha* when describing this place.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:10-13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`); Magadan (ID: `place:Magadan`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Mark 8:11 (id: 21574)

The Pharisees continued to oppose Jesus ([2:16–18](https://ref.ly/Mark2:16-Mark2:18), [24](https://ref.ly/Mark2:24); [3:6](https://ref.ly/Mark3:6); [7:1–5](https://ref.ly/Mark7:1-Mark7:5); [10:2](https://ref.ly/Mark10:2); [12:13](https://ref.ly/Mark12:13), [15](https://ref.ly/Mark12:15)). Their hostility did not stop.

Sometimes, asking for a sign is acceptable ([Judges 6:36–40](https://ref.ly/Judg6:36-Judg6:40); [2 Kings 20:8–11](https://ref.ly/2Kgs20:8-2Kgs20:11); [Isaiah 7:10–12](https://ref.ly/Isa7:10-Isa7:12); see also [John 2:18–19](https://ref.ly/John2:18-John2:19)). But in this case, their request was not sincere. It was a stubborn test of God (they were trying to challenge or trap him).

They were not asking for the kinds of signs Jesus had already done, such as healings, driving out demons (evil spirits), raising the dead, or controlling nature. Many of these had already happened, and people widely knew about them ([Mark 1:32–34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:32-Mark1:34), [45](https://ref.ly/Mark1:45); [3:7–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:7-Mark3:12); [6:53–56](https://ref.ly/Mark6:53-Mark6:56)). Even the Pharisees knew about them ([3:22](https://ref.ly/Mark3:22)). 

Instead, they wanted a sign from heaven (that is, a direct sign from God) to prove once for all that Jesus is the Messiah (God’s chosen king). But no sign would have convinced them.

For those who were open to the truth, the miracles Jesus had already done were clear signs that he is the Messiah, the Son of God ([Luke 7:22](https://ref.ly/Luke7:22)). But for those who were not open, no sign would lead them to believe ([Mark 4:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark4:11-Mark4:12); compare [Luke 6:19–31](https://ref.ly/Luke6:19-Luke6:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Sky (ID: `keyterm:Heaven`)

## Mark 8:12 (id: 21575)

* *Truly I tell you*: This response is very strong. It is like an oath (a serious promise) and means something like, “\[May God judge me] if I give this generation any such sign.” Such statements show firm and unchanging commitment (see [Genesis 14:22–23](https://ref.ly/Gen14:22-Gen14:23); [Numbers 32:10–11](https://ref.ly/Num32:10-Num32:11); [Ruth 1:17](https://ref.ly/Ruth1:17); [1 Samuel 3:17](https://ref.ly/1Sam3:17); [20:13](https://ref.ly/1Sam20:13); [2 Samuel 3:35](https://ref.ly/2Sam3:35); [19:13](https://ref.ly/2Sam19:13)).
* Elsewhere, Jesus describes this generation as "adulterous and sinful" in [Mark 8:38](https://ref.ly/Mark8:38) and "unbelieving" in [9:19](https://ref.ly/Mark9:19).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Symbol (ID: `keyterm:Example`); Spirit (ID: `keyterm:Spirit.2`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`)

## Mark 8:14 (id: 21577)

The mention of having only *one loaf* of bread reminds Mark's audience of earlier times when the disciples did not have enough food. On those occasions, Jesus miraculously provided enough bread to feed thousands of people.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Mark 8:14–21 (id: 21576)

This story of the journey across the Sea of Galilee reflects on the greatness of Jesus, shown in the two feeding miracles. It also emphasizes the disciples’ lack of understanding.

They do not see, hear, or understand because their hearts are hardened (not open to learning; see [4:9–20](https://ref.ly/Mark4:9-Mark4:20); [6:52](https://ref.ly/Mark6:52)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:14-21

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Basket (ID: `realia:Basket`); Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`); Ship (ID: `realia:Ship`)

## Mark 8:15 (id: 21578)

Matthew says that *the leaven of the Pharisees* refers to their false teaching ([Matthew 16:12](https://ref.ly/Matt16:12)). It might also refer to their unbelief and stubbornness ([Mark 8:10–13](https://ref.ly/Mark8:10-Mark8:13)). 

The reference to Herod could suggest he was unwilling to accept what he knew to be true ([6:14](https://ref.ly/Mark6:14), [16](https://ref.ly/Mark6:16), [20](https://ref.ly/Mark6:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:15

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pharisees (ID: `group:Pharisee`); Herod Antipas (ID: `person:Herod.2`)

## Mark 8:16 (id: 21579)

The disciples completely missed the meaning of the warning given by Jesus ([8:15](https://ref.ly/Mark8:15)). They forgot that, because Jesus was with them, their lack of bread did not matter.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:16


## Mark 8:17–20 (id: 21580)

Jesus asked eight questions that rebuked the disciples. They, of all people, should have been aware of the miraculous power of the Son of God. Although the disciples still remembered the feeding miracles, they did not apply their meaning to the situation they were facing.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:17-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Basket (ID: `realia:Basket`); Heart (ID: `keyterm:Heart`)

## Mark 8:19–20 (id: 21581)

The questions of Jesus clearly recalled the feeding miracles ([6:30–44](https://ref.ly/Mark6:30-Mark6:44); [8:1–10](https://ref.ly/Mark8:1-Mark8:10)). He asked the disciples how many baskets of leftovers they had gathered after each event. By asking these questions, Jesus reminded them of what had already happened.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:19-20

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Basket (ID: `realia:Basket`)

## Mark 8:21 (id: 21582)

*Do you still not understand?*: This question suggests there will be a future time when they will truly understand. Mark's audience knew that the resurrection of Jesus would help the disciples understand the identity and power of Jesus (see, for example, [Acts 4:23–31](https://ref.ly/Acts4:23-Acts4:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:21


## Mark 8:22–26 (id: 21583)

* Earlier, Jesus and the disciples tried to go to Bethsaida but did not succeed ([6:45](https://ref.ly/Mark6:45)). Here, they arrive, and Jesus heals a blind man. Only Mark describes this two\-part healing. It might connect to how the disciples came to a new spiritual understanding in the next story ([8:27–38](https://ref.ly/Mark8:27-Mark8:38)).
* [John 1:44](https://ref.ly/John1:44) says Bethsaida is the home of Peter, Andrew, James, and John (see also [John 12:21](https://ref.ly/John12:21)). This small fishing village was officially part of Gaulanitis. But people usually considered it part of Galilee ([John 12:21](https://ref.ly/John12:21)). It was relocated and given status as a city by Philip the Tetrarch (Josephus, *Antiquities* 18\.2\.1\).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:22-26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Bethsaida (ID: `place:Bethsaida`); Ask for (ID: `keyterm:AskFor`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 8:23 (id: 21584)

*He spit on the man’s eyes* (see [7:33](https://ref.ly/Mark7:33); compare with [John 9:6–7](https://ref.ly/John9:6-John9:7)): We do not know exactly what eye problem the man had. Any attempt to identify it is only a guess and can distract from the miracle.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:23


## Mark 8:24–25 (id: 21585)

The healing was not immediate. At first, the man saw imperfectly. After Jesus touched him a second time, he fully healed the man. 

Who is this man who calms the sea and heals the blind? Peter reveals this in the next account. He is the Christ ([8:29](https://ref.ly/Mark8:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:24-25


## Mark 8:26 (id: 21586)

The story ends with Jesus telling the healed man to keep the event a secret (see [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:26

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 8:27–28 (id: 21587)

* Philip the Tetrarch (a regional ruler) was the son of Herod the Great. Philip built Caesarea Philippi on the slopes of Mount Hermon, about 40 kilometers (25 miles) north of the Sea of Galilee. The more famous Caesarea on the coast was built by his father.
* *Who do people say I am?*: As a good teacher, Jesus asked this question to understand what his disciples thought. Their answer is similar to [6:14–16](https://ref.ly/Mark6:14-Mark6:16).
* *John the Baptist*: See study note on 6:14.
* *one of the prophets*: Many people saw Jesus as a prophet (a person who speaks for God; see [6:4](https://ref.ly/Mark6:4); [14:65](https://ref.ly/Mark14:65); [Luke 7:16](https://ref.ly/Luke7:16), [39](https://ref.ly/Luke7:39); [13:33](https://ref.ly/Luke13:33); [24:19](https://ref.ly/Luke24:19)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:27-28
* **Associated Articles:** Mark 6:14 (ID: `21521@TyndaleStudyNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Caesarea Philippi (ID: `place:CaesareaPhilippi`); John (ID: `person:John`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Mark 8:27–38 (id: 21588)

Peter’s declaration that Jesus is the Christ (the Messiah, God’s chosen king) and the first prediction of his death mark a turning point in the Gospel of Mark. This begins a new stage in his ministry. The question, “Who is this?” asked by the disciples in [4:41](https://ref.ly/Mark4:41), is now answered by Peter.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:27-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Caesarea Philippi (ID: `place:CaesareaPhilippi`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Think (ID: `keyterm:Think`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); John (ID: `person:John`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Mark 8:27–9.1 (id: 21589)

This section is the first cycle (a repeated pattern) that includes three parts: 

* Jesus predicts his suffering ([8:31](https://ref.ly/Mark8:31)),
* the disciples respond with misunderstanding ([8:32–33](https://ref.ly/Mark8:32-Mark8:33)), and
* Jesus teaches about discipleship (what it means to follow him; [8:34–9:1](https://ref.ly/Mark8:34-Mark9:1)).

Compare with [9:30–10:31](https://ref.ly/Mark9:30-Mark10:31).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:27-9:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Baptist (ID: `keyterm:Baptist`); Caesarea Philippi (ID: `place:CaesareaPhilippi`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Think (ID: `keyterm:Think`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); John (ID: `person:John`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Mark 8:29–30 (id: 21590)

*Who do you say I am?*: In the Greek text, the word *you* is emphatic. Peter spoke for the group, saying, *You are the Christ*. Peter’s confession was correct, as the command not to tell anyone about him shows (see [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12); see also [1:1](https://ref.ly/Mark1:1); [14:61–62](https://ref.ly/Mark14:61-Mark14:62); [Matthew 16:17](https://ref.ly/Matt16:17)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:29-30

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`)

## Mark 8:31 (id: 21592)

* God thought it necessary for Jesus to suffer (see also [9:11](https://ref.ly/Mark9:11); [13:7](https://ref.ly/Mark13:7), [10](https://ref.ly/Mark13:10)). The elders, leading priests, and teachers of religious law caused his suffering. But it was ultimately God's will (see [10:33](https://ref.ly/Mark10:33); [11:18](https://ref.ly/Mark11:18), [27](https://ref.ly/Mark11:27); [14:1](https://ref.ly/Mark14:1), [43](https://ref.ly/Mark14:43), [53](https://ref.ly/Mark14:53)).
* The death of Jesus would not be the end. Three days later, he would rise from the dead.
* *after three days*: This has the same meaning as "on the third day" and "will be three days and three nights"([Matthew 16:21](https://ref.ly/Matt16:21); [Luke 9:22](https://ref.ly/Luke9:22); [Matthew 12:40](https://ref.ly/Matt12:40)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`)

## Mark 8:31–38 (id: 21591)

*Then He began to teach them that the Son of Man must suffer many things and be rejected by the elders, chief priests, and scribes, and that He must be killed and after three days rise again*: Jesus now clearly predicted what he had previously revealed in a hidden way ([2:19–20](https://ref.ly/Mark2:19-Mark2:20)). In response, Peter objected, so Jesus taught him and the other disciples the nature of his mission and what it really means to follow him.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:31-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Reject (ID: `keyterm:Reject`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); Think (ID: `keyterm:Think`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Mark 8:32 (id: 21593)

Peter understood what Jesus had said, but he did not accept it. Peter shared the popular idea that the Messiah was to be a victorious national ruler, so he thought it made no sense for Jesus to talk of suffering and death.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`)

## Mark 8:33 (id: 21594)

* Jesus looked at his disciples before he corrected Peter in public. He wanted them to understand Peter's mistake.
* *Get behind Me, Satan!*: Jesus does not mean that a demon possessed Peter. Peter spoke from a human perspective, not God's. So without knowing it, he spoke for Satan, "the god of this age" ([2 Corinthians 4:4](https://ref.ly/2Cor4:4)). He repeated Satan's temptation (compare [Matthew 4:8–10](https://ref.ly/Matt4:8-Matt4:10)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:33

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Satan (ID: `deity:Satan`); Think (ID: `keyterm:Think`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 8:34–38 (id: 21595)

* Jesus invites his disciples and the crowd to understand what following him will cost them.
* *must deny himself*:Thismeans letting Jesus decide your goals and purposes in life. Taking up your cross is a metaphor (compare [Luke 9:23](https://ref.ly/Luke9:23)). It means being faithful to Jesus, even to the point of death if necessary. Following the teachings and example of Jesus requires continual commitment.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:34-38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cross (ID: `realia:Cross`)

## Mark 8:35 (id: 21596)

If a person tries *to save his life* by rejecting Jesus, that person *will lose it* in the next world. But if you give your life to Jesus and the spread of the gospel, you *will save it* forever.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:35

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Salvation (ID: `keyterm:Salvation`); Good News (ID: `keyterm:GoodNews`)

## Mark 8:36–37 (id: 21597)

The implied answer to the rhetorical question Jesus asks is that possessing the entire world has no value if you give up eternal life. “He is no fool who gives what he cannot keep to gain that which he cannot lose” (Jim Elliot). You need your soul to enjoy the benefits of the world to come. When you forfeit your life or soul, there is nothing you can give to purchase it back.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:36-37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Life (ID: `keyterm:Life`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Mark 8:38 (id: 21598)

When Jesus, as the Son of Man, returns in glory to judge the world, he will be ashamed of those who were ashamed to be identified with him and his message (compare [Matthew 7:21–23](https://ref.ly/Matt7:21-Matt7:23); [10:32–33](https://ref.ly/Matt10:32-Matt10:33); [Luke 12:8–9](https://ref.ly/Luke12:8-Luke12:9); [2 Timothy 2:12](https://ref.ly/2Tim2:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 8:38

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Glory (ID: `keyterm:Glory`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`); Holy (ID: `keyterm:Holy`); An Angel (ID: `deity:Angel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 9:1 (id: 21612)

*Truly I tell you*: This introductory phrase shows that this promise is important, but people debate what the promise means. It might mean that some disciples will: 

* witness the resurrection of Jesus from the dead (chapter [16](https://ref.ly/Mark16:1-Mark16:20));
* experience the coming of the Holy Spirit upon the church ([Acts 2](https://ref.ly/Acts2:1-Acts2:47));
* witness the destruction of Jerusalem in AD 70; or
* witness the transfiguration of Jesus as a preview of God's coming kingdom.

Mark places this promise right before the transfiguration story ([Mark 9:2–8](https://ref.ly/Mark9:2-Mark9:8)). This suggests he likely considered the transfiguration as its fulfillment (see [2 Peter 1:16–18](https://ref.ly/2Pet1:16-2Pet1:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:1

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Kingdom (ID: `keyterm:Kingdom`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Mark 9:2–8 (id: 21613)

* This story is connected to [8:27–9:1](https://ref.ly/Mark8:27-Mark9:1) both chronologically (*After six days*) and thematically ([9:7](https://ref.ly/Mark9:7)).
* The presence of Peter, James, and John might be the fulfillment of “some standing here” in [9:1](https://ref.ly/Mark9:1) (see also [5:37](https://ref.ly/Mark5:37); [13:3](https://ref.ly/Mark13:3); [14:33](https://ref.ly/Mark14:33)). Some interpreters believe that the Son of God’s glory before he became human (his pre\-incarnate glory) shone through his human nature for a moment at the transfiguration ([John 1:14](https://ref.ly/John1:14); [17:5](https://ref.ly/John17:5)). It is more likely that this event was an early glimpse of the Son of Man’s future glory (see [Matthew 16:28](https://ref.ly/Matt16:28); [2 Peter 1:16–18](https://ref.ly/2Pet1:16-2Pet1:18); compare [Exodus 34:28–35](https://ref.ly/Exod34:28-Exod34:35); [2 Corinthians 3:7–18](https://ref.ly/2Cor3:7-2Cor3:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:2-8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`); James (ID: `person:James`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Clothing (ID: `realia:Clothing`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Mark 9:4 (id: 21635)

The appearance of Elijah and Moses might indicate the fulfillment of the law and the prophets in Jesus (see [Matthew 5:17](https://ref.ly/Matt5:17); compare [Malachi 4:4–5](https://ref.ly/Mal4:4-Mal4:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:4

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`)

## Mark 9:5–6 (id: 21636)

The idea to *put up* *three shelters* as memorials treats Moses, Elijah, and Jesus as equals (see [Leviticus 23:33–43](https://ref.ly/Lev23:33-Lev23:43); [Numbers 29:12–34](https://ref.ly/Num29:12-Num29:34)). But this idea ignored Peter’s earlier confession that Jesus is the Messiah ([Mark 8:29](https://ref.ly/Mark8:29)). It also ignored that only Jesus had been transfigured (his appearance changed and showed his glory, [9:2–3](https://ref.ly/Mark9:2-Mark9:3)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:5-6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Moses (ID: `person:Moses`); Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`); Peter (ID: `person:Peter`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Tent (ID: `realia:Tent`)

## Mark 9:7 (id: 21637)

* *a cloud*: Compare [Exodus 40:34–35](https://ref.ly/Exod40:34-Exod40:35).
* The voice from the cloud gave a strong correction to Peter’s suggestion: *This is My beloved Son*. He is not equal to Moses and Elijah. God’s voice also confirmed what Peter had said earlier ([Mark 8:29](https://ref.ly/Mark8:29)). At the same time, it gently corrected Peter for rejecting the prediction Jesus made about his suffering ([8:32](https://ref.ly/Mark8:32)). God told Peter and the other disciples to listen to Jesus.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:7

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Beloved (ID: `keyterm:Beloved`)

## Mark 9:8 (id: 21638)

Only Jesus remained, not Moses or Elijah. Jesus was clearly superior to Elijah and Moses.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Mark 9:9 (id: 21639)

Secrecy would no longer be needed after the Son of Man had risen from the dead. At that point, people would understand that Jesus the Messiah (God's chosen king) did not come as a political or military leader. Because of this, his message could be announced openly to everyone (see [Acts 2:36](https://ref.ly/Acts2:36); [3:6](https://ref.ly/Acts3:6)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:9


## Mark 9:10 (id: 21603)

The three disciples only understood what Jesus meant by *rise from the dead* after it happened.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:10


## Mark 9:11 (id: 21604)

The disciples wanted to understand the prophecy that Elijah must come first before the Messiah (see [Malachi 4:5–6](https://ref.ly/Mal4:5-Mal4:6)). They wondered how this prophecy fit with the teachings of Jesus about God's kingdom and his own death, resurrection, and second coming ([Mark 8:31](https://ref.ly/Mark8:31),[38](https://ref.ly/Mark8:38); [9:9](https://ref.ly/Mark9:9)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:11

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`)

## Mark 9:12 (id: 21605)

The teachers of the law were correct that Elijah must come before the completion of God’s kingdom (the time when God fully establishes his rule). But Scripture also says that the Son of Man must suffer greatly (for example, [Psalm 22](https://ref.ly/Ps22:1-Ps22:31); [Isaiah 52:13–53:12](https://ref.ly/Isa52:13-Isa53:12)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:12

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`)

## Mark 9:13 (id: 21606)

The teachers of the law made a mistake by not realizing that Elijah had already come (see [Matthew 17:11–13](https://ref.ly/Matt17:11-Matt17:13); compare [Malachi 4:5–6](https://ref.ly/Mal4:5-Mal4:6)). Because of this, they did not recognize that the kingdom of God had already arrived. They also failed to realize that the Messiah's suffering and death, which were predicted in Scripture, were about to take place.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Elijah (ID: `person:Elijah`)

## Mark 9:14 (id: 21608)

Jesus and the three disciples (Peter, James, and John) returned to the other disciples, the crowd, and the teachers of the law. This event is similar to when Moses came down from Mount Sinai to join the people of Israel ([Exodus 34:29–35](https://ref.ly/Exod34:29-Exod34:35)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:14

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Mark 9:14–29 (id: 21607)

This is the fourth and final time Jesus drives out demons in Mark's Gospel ([1:21–28](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21-Mark1:28); [5:1–20](https://ref.ly/Mark5:1-Mark5:20); [7:24–30](https://ref.ly/Mark7:24-Mark7:30)). The disciples could not drive out the demon, so Jesus performed the exorcism. This emphasizes the need for faith (see [2:5](https://ref.ly/Mark2:5); [5:34](https://ref.ly/Mark5:34); [10:52](https://ref.ly/Mark10:52)). This is another example of the great healing ability of Jesus and of the failure of the disciples ([8:32–33](https://ref.ly/Mark8:32-Mark8:33); [9:5–7](https://ref.ly/Mark9:5-Mark9:7)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:14-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); A Father (ID: `person:GenericFather`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`); To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`); Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`); Disbelieve (ID: `keyterm:Disbelieve`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Mark 9:15 (id: 21609)

Some have suggested that just as Moses reflected God’s glory when he descended from the mountain, Jesus’ glorious transfiguration was still evident, so the crowds responded with awe. It is equally possible that Jesus’ arrival after being absent was enough to elicit the awe of the crowd.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:15


## Mark 9:18 (id: 21610)

These symptoms are often associated with epilepsy (see [Matt 17:15](https://ref.ly/Matt17:15)), but Mark emphasizes the demonic origin of the child’s problem ([Mark 9:17–18](https://ref.ly/Mark9:17-Mark9:18), [25–26](https://ref.ly/Mark9:25-Mark9:26), [28](https://ref.ly/Mark9:28)). The disciples’ inability to heal the child magnifies the effect of Jesus’ power ([9:25–26](https://ref.ly/Mark9:25-Mark9:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:18

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Mark 9:19 (id: 21611)

Jesus said to them: Jesus might have been speaking to the disciples, the father ([9:22–24](https://ref.ly/Mark9:22-Mark9:24)), the crowd, or all present. • How long? Their lack of faith was obstinate and hard\-hearted (cp. [3:5](https://ref.ly/Mark3:5); [6:52](https://ref.ly/Mark6:52); [8:17](https://ref.ly/Mark8:17); [10:5](https://ref.ly/Mark10:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:19

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be Unfaithful (ID: `keyterm:Unfaithful`)

## Mark 9:20 (id: 21614)

The demon did not speak (compare [1:24](https://ref.ly/Mark1:24); [5:7](https://ref.ly/Mark5:7), [9–10](https://ref.ly/Mark5:9-Mark5:10); see also [1:34](https://ref.ly/Mark1:34); [3:11–12](https://ref.ly/Mark3:11-Mark3:12)). Instead, it acted violently, just as the father had described ([9:18](https://ref.ly/Mark9:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:20


## Mark 9:21–22 (id: 21615)

* The demon’s activity had been taking place since he was a little boy*,* and the demon had tried to kill the child in various ways.
* *If You can*: The father’s request shows that his faith had become weak ([9:18](https://ref.ly/Mark9:18)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:21-22

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** A Father (ID: `person:GenericFather`); Affection (ID: `keyterm:Affection`)

## Mark 9:23 (id: 21616)

Jesus urged the man to trust in the power of God. Anything is possible if a person believes (see [11:22–24](https://ref.ly/Mark11:22-Mark11:24)). But a lack of faith can limit how people receive God’s saving work (see [6:5](https://ref.ly/Mark6:5)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:23

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Mark 9:24 (id: 21617)

In desperation, the father cried out that he believed, though weakly.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:24

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Disbelieve (ID: `keyterm:Disbelieve`); A Father (ID: `person:GenericFather`); Believe (ID: `keyterm:Believe`)

## Mark 9:25 (id: 21618)

The crowd was approaching, so Jesus quickly proceeded with the exorcism. • The I in the command to the demon is emphatic, dramatizing Jesus’ authority and power. • never enter him again! At times exorcisms could be temporary (see [Matt 12:43–45](https://ref.ly/Matt12:43-Matt12:45); [Luke 11:24–26](https://ref.ly/Luke11:24-Luke11:26)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:25

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Command (ID: `keyterm:Command`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`)

## Mark 9:26–27 (id: 21619)

The demon reacted with great force. This showed how powerful the demon was and how much greater Jesus was (compare [1:25–26](https://ref.ly/Mark1:25-Mark1:26); [5:15](https://ref.ly/Mark5:15)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:26-27

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); A Group of People (ID: `group:GenericGroup`)

## Mark 9:28–29 (id: 21620)

The story ends in an unexpected way. It emphasizes the importance of prayer more than the importance of faith (compare [11:22–24](https://ref.ly/Mark11:22-Mark11:24)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:28-29

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Pray (ID: `keyterm:Pray`); Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`); A Group of Disciples (ID: `group:GenericDisciples`); House (ID: `realia:House`); Temple (ID: `realia:Temple`)

## Mark 9:30–10.31 (id: 21621)

This section is the second cycle (see study note on 8:27–9:1) containing:

* a prediction about the suffering of Jesus ([9:30–32](https://ref.ly/Mark9:30-Mark9:32)),
* an error by the disciples ([9:33–34](https://ref.ly/Mark9:33-Mark9:34)), and
* teaching about discipleship ([9:35–10:31](https://ref.ly/Mark9:35-Mark10:31)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:30-10:31

## Mark 9:31 (id: 21622)

* Jesus continued to seek privacy to teach the disciples about his future suffering (see [7:24](https://ref.ly/Mark7:24)).
* *The Son of Man will be delivered*: See [14:10–11](https://ref.ly/Mark14:10-Mark14:11), [41–45](https://ref.ly/Mark14:41-Mark14:45).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:31

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Be a Disciple (ID: `keyterm:Disciple`)

## Mark 9:32 (id: 21623)

The disciples were still unable to accept the teaching of Jesus concerning his death or to recognize how it fit into God’s plan.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:32

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Fear (ID: `keyterm:Fear`); Word (ID: `keyterm:Word`)

## Mark 9:33–34 (id: 21624)

* Capernaum was where Jesus lived in Galilee ([1:21](https://ref.ly/Mark1:21); [2:1](https://ref.ly/Mark2:1)).
* The disciples of Jesus did not answer his question because they knew he would not approve of their discussion.
* *arguing about which of them was the greatest*: This issue would come up again in [10:35–37](https://ref.ly/Mark10:35-Mark10:37).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:33-34

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Capernaum (ID: `place:Capernaum`); House (ID: `realia:House`)

## Mark 9:33–50 (id: 21625)

The disciples did not understand the prediction Jesus made about his suffering. Because of this, they could not see what it meant for their own lives.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:33-50

## Mark 9:35 (id: 21626)

*Sitting down*: Jesus sat down and took the role of a teacher (see [4:1–2](https://ref.ly/Mark4:1-Mark4:2); [12:41](https://ref.ly/Mark12:41); [Matthew 5:1](https://ref.ly/Matt5:1); [Luke 4:20](https://ref.ly/Luke4:20); [5:3](https://ref.ly/Luke5:3)). 

True greatness in the kingdom of God means serving others, not ruling over them. Jesus showed this by his own example ([Mark 10:45](https://ref.ly/Mark10:45)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:35


## Mark 9:36–37 (id: 21627)

In the time of Jesus, people did not view children as especially innocent or pure. Instead, they saw them as weak and of low status. Jesus said that people should receive a child in his name (that is, as his representative).

Welcoming a child shows humility. It means choosing a low place and serving others ([9:35](https://ref.ly/Mark9:35); see also [10:13–16](https://ref.ly/Mark10:13-Mark10:16)). Other teachings of Jesus show that how people receive or reject his followers is the same as how they receive or reject him ([Matthew 10:40](https://ref.ly/Matt10:40); [25:31–46](https://ref.ly/Matt25:31-Matt25:46); [Luke 10:16](https://ref.ly/Luke10:16); [John 13:20](https://ref.ly/John13:20)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:36-37

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`)

## Mark 9:38–41 (id: 21628)

An unknown exorcist was casting out demons in Jesus’ name though he was not one of the twelve disciples ([9:38–40](https://ref.ly/Mark9:38-Mark9:40)). John objected, but Jesus corrected him. • we told him to stop: Cp. [10:35–43](https://ref.ly/Mark10:35-Mark10:43); [Luke 9:51–55](https://ref.ly/Luke9:51-Luke9:55). • The success of the unknown exorcist is contrasted with the failure of the disciples in the previous account ([Mark 9:14–29](https://ref.ly/Mark9:14-Mark9:29)).

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:38-41

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Name (ID: `keyterm:Name`); Jesus (ID: `person:Jesus.2`); Demons (ID: `deity:Demons`); John (ID: `person:John.2`); Surely! (ID: `keyterm:Surely`); Ability (ID: `keyterm:Ability`)

## Mark 9:40 (id: 21629)

The negative parallel to this proverb is found in [Luke 11:23](https://ref.ly/Luke11:23). These two proverbs should be understood as complementary, not contradictory, for Luke includes them both ([Luke 9:50](https://ref.ly/Luke9:50); [11:23](https://ref.ly/Luke11:23)). The exorcist casting out demons in Jesus’ name was not against Jesus, but for him.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:40


## Mark 9:42–50 (id: 21630)

if you cause . . . sin: The consequences of causing sin ties these verses together ([9:42–43](https://ref.ly/Mark9:42-Mark9:43), [45](https://ref.ly/Mark9:45), [47](https://ref.ly/Mark9:47)). These sayings were not meant to be interpreted literally, for carrying them out would not prevent sin (see [Matt 15:10–20](https://ref.ly/Matt15:10-Matt15:20)). What Jesus conveys by these warnings is that no sin is worth going to hell for. It is better to repent—even if repenting is as painful as cutting off a hand, foot, or eye—than to suffer the punishment of hell.

* **Associated Passages:** Mark 9:42-50
